> **⚠️ Before starting ANY row — Existing-Work-First (CONTRIBUTING.md § Existing-Work-First Policy):** `git status` + `git log` first (uncommitted/fresh work matching the row = it's done or in progress — finish it, don't redo it); check for a second live session (`ps aux | grep claude`) before editing shared files; grep to *disprove* the row's premise (rows go stale — four rows have closed as ALREADY SHIPPED). #### §CODEX-01 — The read-only Codex: surface what the engine already knows (merges §POT-W1 + §POT-S4 + §POT-M1) 🔴

**The finding (measured).** The game has **no rules surface of any kind** — `grep -ci "helpScreen|rulesTab|glossary|codexScreen|showRules|showHelp"` = **0** across 37,870 lines. The entire tutorial is **one NPC monologue** (`yael.impartial[0]`), and §PLAY-01-D had to add a special-case guarantee (`yaelOnboardingSeen`) purely to deliver it once because the auto-active Slums quest shadowed it. Meanwhile the engine privately knows a lot it never transmits: `_magicTierAllowed` = `level ≥ magic×5`, `XP_LEVELS`, `CONDITION_ADV`, `_monsterLevel`, `EFFORT_XP_PCT` (`const EFFORT_XP_PCT@24261`), the d100 loot weights. Two neighbours have the same shape: **downtime verbs exist with no hub** (`downtime` = 0 hits, but fishing with 20 ranks + `FISHING_GUIDE_TEXT` (`const FISHING_GUIDE_TEXT@26489`), 5 vendor nodes, **38** `sleep:true` rest nodes, Hunt Mode, 12 `type:'craft'` quests all ship — the game never tells you a place *offers* them); and **world lore lives only as prose** — `WORLD_DB` (`const WORLD_DB@6279`) is `{label,icon,monsters[]}` with no political/historical field, and **219 `meta.worldTruth` lines** now render only as the §NPC-01-C `✦` footer at Dear Friend (fav≥2), so most are still unread at any reachable favor.

**The thesis (why one surface, not three).** This is the **textbook §PLAY-01 "honesty" class** — *the engine knows things it won't transmit* — the exact pattern §PLAY-01-A proved safe (the objective chip: surface the known, diegetically, UI-only, **zero balance risk, no design call**). `potential.md` §POT-M1 explicitly says *"Merge with §POT-W1 + §POT-S4 into one codex surface."* Do **not** add `culture:`/`power:` fields to `WORLD_DB` first — that is authoring, and the content already exists unread; this track is a **renderer**, not new lore.

**Increments (each a shippable slice):**
- [ ] **§CODEX-01-A — the Codex shell + Rules/Glossary tab (keystone).** A read-only overlay (mirror the objective-chip / void-modal UI patterns) with a Rules pane that reads the engine's *own* constants live — skill-check formula, magic-tier rule, XP curve, effort-XP %, loot weights, `TIER_LABELS`. No new data: it reflects existing constants so it can never drift from balance.
- [ ] **§CODEX-01-B — Gazetteer pane (folds §POT-W1).** Per-region lore aggregated from already-authored strings — `NODE_MAP[].text` + the region's `worldTruth` lines — as a browsable read-only index. Still a renderer; authors nothing.
- [ ] **§CODEX-01-C — "What this place offers" downtime board (folds §POT-S4).** At the current node, list the verbs that actually apply here (rest + cost, vendor, fishing if a rod-node, craft, Hunt) by querying node/state — the honest "you have time; here's what's here" surface §POT-S4 wants.

**Invariants:** render-only — **Free-Movement untouched** (no mover code, no quest-state in a step); outside all four parity fences (no `js/*.js` twin — inline engine JS, edited directly); **seeded-RNG N/A** (no rolls). ⚠️ Hazard #1 (inline-JS hand-edit — stop the server first, commit early). **Verify:** new `tests/integration/codex.test.js` (overlay opens/closes; Rules pane reflects the live constants; Gazetteer lists ≥1 `worldTruth`; downtime board matches the node's real verbs) + eyeball (§7½). Baseline reds unchanged (I1/I2, R2/R3).

#### §TIDE-01 — The world visibly changes after events (merges §POT-P2 + §POT-W5 + §POT-W4) 🔴

**The finding (measured).** §GR shipped `node.textVariants:[{flag,text}]` (`85faf9b`) — recorded as *"display-only, API-authored, **reusable for any 'node changes after an event'."*** The renderer is generic and idle: `_renderNodeText`-side loop at `node.textVariants@31750` (`for (const _v of (node.textVariants||[]))… if (S_story[_v.flag]) …`). Measured consumers: **exactly one** (`flag:'fishmongerRowRestored'@8689`, the §GR payoff). So a **built, tested, API-authorable mechanism has 1 of 401 possible consumers.** Two beats want it and have never used it: **(W5) the apocalypse is narrated in past tense and never becomes world state** — `VOID_TIDE_EVENTS` (`const VOID_TIDE_EVENTS@22203`) narrate the Void's advance and `_addVoidPressure` (`function _addVoidPressure@26805`, called on sleep at `Increment void pressure at each tide event@35846`) advances a counter, but **no node has ever become ruined**; and **(W4) the 1367-vs-cyberpunk blend is unjustified in-world** — §1367 already fixed this class once (Events A–F were parked at the HKG cyberpunk node and had to be relocated to thematic nodes).

**The design (already legal, already implemented).** CONTRIBUTING's Free-Movement policy **explicitly blesses W5's shape**: *"If a future feature needs a place to feel impassable until something happens, it must do so by **changing terrain/`CELL_GRID`/`IMPASSABLE_CELLS` state** (the cell genuinely becomes/stops-being sea), not by consulting quest state inside the mover."* `IMPASSABLE_CELLS` is a computed set (`const IMPASSABLE_CELLS@9758`) → the Void tide rewrites *terrain/cell state* (the cell genuinely changes), never gates a step. W4/W5 node-text beats are **pure authoring against shipped rails** — `textVariants` needs **zero code**.

**Increments:**
- [x] **§TIDE-01-A — the W4 anachronism account (zero code) ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-23.** One `textVariants` beat giving the 1367-courier-in-Neon-Undercity blend a diegetic account, keyed on an existing flag. Pure `./api.sh` authoring; proves the idle renderer on a second consumer. **✅ Done:** added `textVariants:[{flag:'cyMaintenanceDecoded', text:…}]` to **HKG** (Neon Undercity, `8328`), keyed on the existing flag set by **`quest_d0207_a4`** ("The Cipher" — the moment the player decodes that a **Scholar King** built the Undercity three centuries ago, matching the Froberger cipher fragment). The variant re-reads the same descent (30 steps · forced turnstiles · two androids + data wraith checkpoint · Bruhns already past — all functional beats preserved) with the neon reframed as the Scholar King's **bioluminescent resonance channels**, so the anachronism reads as ancient engineering, not a genre error. Deliberately keyed on `cyMaintenanceDecoded` (a4), **not** `cyOriginKnown` (a5), so it does **not** spoil the deeper "the builder's name is *yours*" reveal. **Write path:** offline one-shot through the **WBAPI core** (`WBAPI.load → editStructuredField('node','HKG','textVariants',…) → save(GAME)`) — byte-identical to what the server's `PUT /api/node/HKG` does (server routes array fields to `editStructuredField` at `wbapi-server.js:10958`), invoked in-process to honor **no-background-processes** (no server spun up). **Verified:** WBAPI roundtrip ok (len 1448, `inserted:true`); git diff = **HKG +1 line only**; inline `<script>` parse **0 errors**; new `tide-node-textvariants.test.js` **3/3** (flag-unset shows base + not variant; `cyMaintenanceDecoded:true` shows variant + not base; AMS first-consumer regression still swaps → rail is generic); `check:invariants` — **2 pre-existing J14/J15 junction violations, unchanged** (confirmed identical at HEAD via stash; **not mine** — logged as §JUNK-01 below). Not committed (user rule).
- [x] **§JUNK-01 ✅ CLOSED 2026-07-28 via §DX-01a** — option (b)-adjacent: J14/J15 removed outright (§WALK's 0-junction model restored) and the road they stood in for actually laid (`build-roads.js --apply`); `check:invariants` I1/I2 + `check:roads` R2/R3 all green. Full record: the §DX-01a ship row. Original row: **2 pre-existing invariant violations: J14/J15 junction nodes reference a `junction` terrain absent from `WORLD_DB` (found during §TIDE-01-A).** `check:invariants` reports **I2** (`junction:true=2` occurrences in source) + **I1** (`J14→junction`, `J15→junction` — 2 nodes reference a terrain absent from `WORLD_DB`). **Confirmed pre-existing** at HEAD (`git stash` diff — identical count before/after the §TIDE-01-A edit), so not introduced by recent work. Either (a) add a `junction` terrain entry to `WORLD_DB` (matching the `junction:true` node convention), or (b) reassign J14/J15 to a real terrain + drop the `junction:true` marker per §WALK's junction model (0 junctions was the §WALK close-out claim — these 2 may be strays that survived). **Terrain/graph change → via WBAPI**, and re-run `check:invariants` (target: I1/I2 clean) + `check:roads`. Low urgency (both are junctions, not player-facing settlements), but it's the only thing keeping `check:invariants` red.
- [ ] **§TIDE-01-B — Void-tide node-text ruin (authoring).** Wire `textVariants` on the nodes `VOID_TIDE_EVENTS` names, keyed on the Void-pressure flags that already advance, so a place *reads* as ruined once the tide reaches it. Still zero engine code.
- [ ] **§TIDE-01-C — Void-tide terrain rewrite (the real mechanism; **lab report first**).** Have the tide flip specific cells' terrain/`IMPASSABLE_CELLS` state when pressure crosses a threshold — the genuine "the area was altered by [event]" beat. ⚠️ This touches cell/terrain state + reachability → **lock the cell list + thresholds in a lab report**, and **re-run `check:reachability` (100% from LHR) + `check:roads` after** (a rewrite must not orphan a settlement). **Trap:** change terrain state, never gate the mover.

**Invariants:** A/B are data-only (Free-Movement N/A). C changes terrain/cell state **only via `IMPASSABLE_CELLS`/`CELL_GRID`** — never quest-state in the mover; **no jump travel**; reachability must stay 100%. ⚠️ Restart the server before the write session (Hazard #1). **Verify:** A/B — eyeball the node text after the flag flips (§7½); C — `check:reachability`/`check:roads`/`check:invariants` green + `check:walk` + eyeball. Sync `world.md` §Void Tide + `maps.md`/`story.md` for any cell change.

#### §XP-02 — First-arrival exploration XP (completes §XP-01) 🟠

**The finding (measured).** §XP-01's user directive was *"all action earns XP"* and it shipped **three** grant sites — fled enemy, missed attack, failed check (`EFFORT_XP_PCT = 0.25`, `const EFFORT_XP_PCT@24261`; fail-XP at `a failed check still earns effort XP@6891`). Yet **exploration — the one thing the game is built around — grants nothing.** `S_story.visited` is a bare boolean per node whose **only** consumer is map tinting (`_getNodeMapColor`, `function _getNodeMapColor@27392`; `.visited` = 17 refs, all cosmetic). Walking somewhere new earns nothing.

**The design.** This is an **unfinished §XP-01**, not new mechanics: add a fourth grant site keyed on *first* arrival at a node (guard on the existing `visited` boolean flipping false→true) using §XP-01's existing dials. **Bound it** so it can't out-earn combat (mirror §XP-01's cap: exploration XP ≤ the flee value) and so backtracking pays nothing (the `visited` guard already gives first-arrival-only for free). No seeded roll needed (a flat/level-scaled grant), so **RNG N/A**.

**Increments:**
- [x] **§XP-02-A — first-arrival grant ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-23.** On the `visited[node]` false→true transition, award a small bounded XP grant; make it level-aware if desired (a trivial node at L18 pays little) but keep it flat-simple first. **✅ Done:** new dial `const EXPLORE_XP = 10` (next to `EFFORT_XP_PCT`/`EFFORT_MISS_PCT`, `~24070`) + `_grantExplorationXp(node)` helper, hooked in `storyCollectLoot` (`~29655`, the sole `visited`-setting site) as `if (!S_story.visited[node.code]) _grantExplorationXp(node)` — **before** the visited flip, so it fires exactly once per node; backtracking pays nothing (the guard), and a `if (node.junction) return` guard skips routing junctions. XP is granted **inside `storyRender` after the free step already landed** (never gates movement); UI (message + any level-up modal) is **deferred via `setTimeout`** so the render finishes first, and `_levelUpQueue` is sliced-captured before the deferred read. Accrues telemetry to `S_story.explorationXp`. **Bounded** ≤ the weakest starter flee value (10 ≤ protofleder `round(12·16·0.25)`=48), so exploring can never out-earn a fight. **RNG N/A** (flat grant, `check:rng` green). **Verify:** new `exploration-xp.test.js` **2/2** (first arrival grants EXPLORE_XP once; re-visit grants 0; bounded ≤ protofleder flee; e2e telemetry through the real render path); inline `<script>` parse 0 errors. **Regression caught + fixed:** the full integration suite surfaced **1** real break — `quest-runtime-uqf.test.js:9509` (§MATH-01 "arrival at ZERO … xp+gold") calls `storyCollectLoot` on an unvisited node and asserted `xp===350+500`; now correctly `350+500+EXPLORE_XP` (the test was proven to be MY regression by passing on HEAD via stash-diff; the sibling arrival tests at `9538`/`9560` assert only gold/state, unaffected). **Full suite = 635 passed**; the 4 `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` + 1 flaky `multiplayer-presence` failures were **confirmed pre-existing** (identical on HEAD; worldbuilder tests never touch `roll2hit-v3.html`). `check:walk` sub-checks parity/behaviour/terrain/roomsparity/questparity/gateast/rng/questgraph all **green**; only the **pre-existing** `check:invariants` (J14/J15 → §JUNK-01) + `check:roads` (R3 sea-overlap + 3 off-component settlements = the §DX-01 TGS/SPB baseline) stay red, both **unchanged** by this edit. Docs synced: `mechanics.md` §XP (three-dial table) + `docs/mechanics/mechanics-combat.md`. Not committed (user rule).
- [ ] **§XP-02-B (optional) — a surfaced "unexplored" signal.** Pairs with §CODEX-01/§POT-H2's fog-of-war idea; defer unless wanted (no fog-of-war state today — do **not** add one speculatively).

**Invariants:** Free-Movement untouched (XP is granted *after* the free step lands, never gates it); outside all parity fences; if any randomness is ever added it draws the seeded stream (`_seededNext()`), never `Math.random()`. **Verify:** `tests/integration/exploration-xp.test.js` (first arrival grants once; a re-visit grants nothing; total exploration XP ≤ the §XP-01 cap) + `check:walk`. Sync `mechanics.md` §XP + `docs/mechanics/`.

#### §CLUE-01 — Cumulative multi-clue mysteries (no new opcode) 🟠

**The finding (measured).** Mysteries are the file's best content (`quest_scar_01` "The Open Ledger"), but the shape is always **one `skill_check` → pass/fail → done** — no clue accumulation, no partial knowledge, because `execBits` is straight-line and cannot carry a result forward. **But the parts already ship:** `S_story.knowledge[]` is a clue store (the `reward` bit's `knowledge` field), and knowledge items already render — `.inv-item-knowledge` CSS exists (`.inv-item-knowledge@1546`, `.inv-item-knowledge@3238`). A **cumulative** mystery needs no VM change; only a **branching** one needs §VM-01.

**The design.** Author multi-clue mysteries as *existing* UQF: several quests each granting a `knowledge` clue, then a final quest gated `{all:[flags/knowledge…]}` (or `{any}`) that delivers the reveal `narrative`. **No new opcode, no `execBits` change** — this is a **content pattern demonstrated end-to-end**, not an engine track. ⚠️ Do **not** hand-author a *branching* chain: `skill_check` "smuggles its own branch" and CONTRIBUTING names it *"the exception to retire, not the pattern to copy."* Cumulative-only here.

**Increments:**
- [ ] **§CLUE-01-A — confirm + document the pattern.** `./api.sh advise` on a scratch clue quest; write the canonical "cumulative mystery" recipe into `quest.md` (like the "collect at node" pattern) so future mysteries reuse it.
- [ ] **§CLUE-01-B — one exemplar arc.** Author a 3–4-clue mystery (clues via `knowledge` grants at distinct nodes → a `{all}`-gated reveal), UQF-only, with an integration test. Lab report only if it grows past a handful of quests.

**Invariants:** UQF data only — Host/Script separation kept (no new bit kind, no `_legacy_fn`); Free-Movement N/A (gates list missions, never block steps); seeded RNG for any check. **Verify:** `./api.sh advise` clean on each quest + `tests/integration/clue-mystery.test.js` (clues accumulate in `knowledge[]`; the reveal gate opens only when all are held) + `check:walk` (`check:questgraph` proves no soft-lock). Sync `quest.md`.

#### §BOSS-01 — Declarative boss phases + terrain-conditioned encounters (merges §POT-B3 + §POT-B2) 🟠

**The finding (measured).** `EPIC_BOSS_POOL` (10 refs) is **20 flat stat blocks** — no phases (`grep -c "phases:"` = **0**). The only staging is §PLAY-01-B's **one-time enrage at ≤30% HP**, tier-scaled, guarded by `S.opp.enraged` (reset `// §PLAY-01-B: one-time low-HP Void enrage@24474`, fire `S.opp.enraged = true@25046`). And **terrain is in the room but never in the fight**: no legendary/lair actions, no environmental advantage — combat reads terrain only via the `_VOID_TERRAIN_RE` name heuristic; the `ROOMS` kernel knows `terrainAt` but the fight never asks.

**The design (generalize shipped code into data).** Both halves have a **ready-made safe pattern**: §PLAY-01-B added its AI branch in `_storyEnemyTurn` (`function _storyEnemyTurn@25026`) — **outside the kernel** — leaving `DUEL:CORE` (`DUEL:CORE:START@10125`) git-diff-verified untouched. Do the same here.
- **B3 → `phases`:** a declarative `phases:[{atPct, atk, dmg, msg}]` field on `EPIC_BOSS_POOL` generalizes the enrage (which **is** a one-stage phase with working `S.opp.enraged` lifecycle) into data — squarely Host/Script-Separation-compliant (new shapes go in the grammar/data, never a new single-use term), reusing the enrage lifecycle rather than inventing one.
- **B2 → terrain-conditioned encounters:** read `terrainAt`/the node's terrain in `_storyEnemyTurn` (outside `DUEL:CORE`) to grant an environmental modifier, mirroring §PLAY-01-B's branch site.

**Increments:**
- [ ] **§BOSS-01-A — `phases` field (B3).** Add the declarative field + a phase-transition check in the enrage site; migrate the existing enrage to be `phases:[{atPct:30,…}]` so current behavior is byte-identical, then author a 2-phase boss.
- [ ] **§BOSS-01-B — terrain modifier in `_storyEnemyTurn` (B2).** A small, data-described terrain→encounter effect, kept entirely outside the kernel.

**Invariants:** ⚠️ **`DUEL:CORE` (`DUEL:CORE:START@10125`) is parity-fenced** — never edit the inlined copy; the effect lives in `_storyEnemyTurn` (host, outside the fence); if `js/duel.js` is touched at all, re-inline + `scripts/check-duel-parity.js`. Enrage/phase rolls draw the **seeded** stream. Free-Movement N/A. **Verify:** `tests/integration/boss-phases.test.js` (enrage-as-phase byte-identical; a 2-phase boss transitions at each `atPct`; terrain modifier applies only on the right terrain) + `scripts/check-duel-parity.js` green (kernel untouched) + `check:walk`. Sync `monsters.md` (`EPIC_BOSS_POOL` `phases` shape) + `docs/mechanics/mechanics-combat.md`.

#### §CHAR-01 — One-time point-buy at character creation ✅ ALREADY SHIPPED 2026-07-23 (the §POT-C1 open slice) 🟢

> **⚠️ CORRECTION (2026-07-23) — §CHAR-01-A was ALREADY SHIPPED; the "design call" is moot. grep-before-building win.** The finding below claimed `grep -c "pointBuy|standardArray|_ASI_LEVELS" = 0` and concluded "the player never chooses them." **That grep used the wrong terms.** The shipped point-buy is named `CC_BUDGET`/`_CC_COST`/`_cc_scores`/`_showCharCreate`/`story-charcreate-modal`. Measured live: a complete **27-point D&D-5e point-buy** (`_CC_COST=[0,1,2,3,4,5,7,9]` for scores 8..15, `CC_BUDGET=27`, `37748`) in the **"⚔ Choose Your Origin"** modal (`4960`) with **custom + hard** origin tabs, +/- steppers clamped 8–15 with live budget enforcement (`_ccRefresh`/`_ccSpent`), **wired** `btn-continue-new`/`btn-gameover-new` → `_showCharCreate()` → `btn-charcreate-begin` → `storyNewGame(scores)` (`37816`) which writes `S_story.abilityScores` + derives `atkBonus`/`hpMax`. So the player DOES author the character the 2,631 checks are about. It just had **no test** — now locked by `char-creation.test.js` **4/4** (budget=27 + cost curve; modal + custom/hard tabs + begin button; custom Begin writes scores & derives ATK/HP; hard Begin ships the fixed array). **Same class as §GR/§DESIGN-03/§DUNGEON-01/§1367/§MATH-01 — "planned" work that was already live; grepping the real symbol names (not the seed's guessed ones) closed it.** Optional residual only: add an explicit **standard-array `[15,14,13,12,10,8]` preset** button as a third origin (convenience, not a fork) — logged as §CHAR-01-B below; the core one-time point-buy needs nothing.

**The finding (measured — SUPERSEDED by the correction above).** Fighter-only is **explicit design** (`FIGHTER_FEATURES`; the L10 entry: *"No style choice in this world — your body has become the weapon"*) and races/classes **must not** be built — they would invalidate the DC curve across 2,850 quests. **But** `abilityScores` (STR/DEX/CON/INT/WIS/CHA) drive **all 2,631 `skill_check` bits** via `_rollSkill` — the player DOES choose them at new game via the shipped point-buy (the original claim of "never chooses" was a wrong-search-term artifact, see correction).

**The design (honest, bounded, no rebalance).** A **one-time point-buy / standard-array at `storyNewGame()`** — no class, no race, **no DC rebalance** (total points fixed so the curve is unchanged in aggregate; a player merely *distributes* what was already implicitly flat). Fits `_S_DEFAULTS()` per §STATE-INIT (declare nothing new — the field exists; just let new-game write it). **Trap:** keep the point total balanced against the existing DC curve — this is redistribution, not power creep.

**Increments:**
- [x] **§CHAR-01-A — the point-buy screen at new game ✅ ALREADY SHIPPED (verified 2026-07-23).** The one-time allocation UI at `storyNewGame()` exists and is wired (see the correction above): the 27-point-buy "⚔ Choose Your Origin" modal writes `S_story.abilityScores`, guarded to fresh new game only (`_showCharCreate` clears autosave via `storyNewGame`'s `_S_DEFAULTS()` reset, respecting §STATE-INIT's single-source shape). **No design call was needed** — point-buy is the live system. Locked by `char-creation.test.js` 4/4. Not committed (user rule).
- [ ] **§CHAR-01-B (optional) — a standard-array preset origin.** Add a third origin button seeding `_cc_scores = {15,14,13,12,10,8}` (the classic 5e array) as a one-click convenience next to custom/hard. Pure additive UI, same `storyNewGame` path, no rebalance. Low priority — the point-buy already covers this allocation manually.

**Invariants:** writes an **existing** `_S_DEFAULTS()` field (no new persistent field); DC curve unchanged (fixed point total); Free-Movement/parity N/A; no rolls (RNG N/A). ⚠️ Hazard #1 (inline-JS). **Verify:** `tests/integration/point-buy.test.js` (new game writes a valid distribution; total matches the fixed budget; a mid-game save is untouched) + `check:walk`. Sync `mechanics.md` §Character + `index.md` State Fields. **Ask-gate:** the array-vs-point-buy choice is a user design call (Loop-vs-Ask).

#### §MESH-01-HIRE — Give the hireling a voice (the §POT-C4 open slice) 🟢

**The finding (measured).** No party — and none wanted (`DUEL:CORE`/`S_story`/XP are single-combatant; MESH multiplayer is the better answer and ships). But the **§MESH-01g hired-guide bot** (`#hireling-section`, `hireling-section@2862`+) is *"a single-player companion, daily fee"* with **no `NPC_DIALOGUES` entry** — the one "companion" in the game is a daily charge with no voice.

**The design.** Give the hireling a dialogue key so it becomes a character, not a fee. Tiny, self-contained. Optionally surface a line when hired/paid.

**Increments:**
- [ ] **§MESH-01-HIRE-A — a dialogue key for the guide bot.** Add an `NPC_DIALOGUES` entry (with `meta`) for the hireling and render a line at hire/daily-fee time.

**Invariants:** data + a small render hook — Free-Movement/parity/RNG all N/A. ⚠️ Hazard #1 if the render hook is inline JS. **Verify:** eyeball the hire flow (§7½) + a smoke assertion that the line renders. Sync `story.md`/`docs/story/story-arc-npc-dialogues.md`.

#### §RIPPLE-01 — Give the favor & mission-bit tracks consumers (merges §POT-P3 + §POT-W3) 🟠

**The finding (measured).** Two near-dead relationship tracks have no *consequences*: **(P3)** `mission_bit` is the second-most-authored opcode — **2,448 receipts** (`_flagToLabel` rewrite `function _flagToLabel@25916`+, `token.day` timeline, `_takeMissionBit` gate-safety all shipped) — but they are read **only by gates** (i.e. only to decide whether a *later mission lists*); nothing ever *reacts* to a token you carry. **(W3)** rest is **identical at all 38 `sleep:true` nodes** and costs nothing; the file has a full favor model that a haven could hang on, but a haven's quality never tracks its resident NPC's favor.

**The design (now unblocked by §NPC-01-B).** §NPC-01-B took the NPC card from ~20 → ~203 reachable NPCs — so **NPCs now have a render surface to react *on***. P3: render a reaction line on an NPC's card when you carry a relevant mission-bit token (a receipt they'd recognize). W3: let a `sleep:true` haven's quality (rest message / a small bonus) track the resident NPC's favor — legal because **resting is not movement** (no quest/flag refuses a step). Both give the 16-bit favor track + the 2,448-token ledger their first *reactive* consumer.

**Increments:**
- [ ] **§RIPPLE-01-A — NPC reacts to a carried token (P3).** On the NPC card, when the player holds a mission-bit the NPC is keyed to, show a recognition line. Render-only; reuses §NPC-01's card. **Depends on §NPC-01-B (shipped).**
- [ ] **§RIPPLE-01-B — haven quality tracks resident favor (W3).** The rest message (and optionally a tiny rest bonus) at a `sleep:true` node varies with the resident NPC's favor. **Trap:** resting only — never touch the mover.
- [ ] **§RIPPLE-01-C (design call) — widen the favor track.** With ~203 card-bearing NPCs, the 16-`favor`-bit track is too thin to earn the ladder; overlaps **§NPC-01-D**. Coordinate — do not double-scope.

**Invariants:** render + rest only — **Free-Movement untouched** (resting is not a step); outside all parity fences; RNG N/A (or seeded for any bonus roll). ⚠️ Hazard #1 (inline JS). **Verify:** `tests/integration/npc-token-reaction.test.js` (card shows the line only when the token is held) + a rest-quality assertion + eyeball (§7½). Sync `story.md`/`docs/story/story-arc-npc-dialogues.md`. **Coordinate with §NPC-01-D** on the favor-widening.

#### §MUD-01 — A text/MUD-style view over the shipped room engine (the §POT-S3 unblocked half) 🟡

**The finding (measured).** `ROOMS:CORE`'s `describeCell` (`function describeCell@10037`; kernel `ROOMS:CORE:START@9872`) already produces full room prose + per-direction exits with `__roadDestination` hints — but its full output is exercised mainly by `tests/mud-harness.mjs` (**269/269**); the player sees only node text + the §NAV-01-FU road-hover tooltip. *"Describe a vivid environment"* is close to what `describeCell` **already returns.**

**The design.** A text/MUD-style view is **mostly a renderer over shipped output** — surface `describeCell`'s prose + exits as an in-game panel. The *"I declare how my character interacts"* half is **§POT-R1 (BLOCKED on §VM-01 Inc A)** and is explicitly **out of scope** — this track ships only the **read** half (the view), which is unblocked and cheap.

**Increments:**
- [ ] **§MUD-01-A — the descriptive view.** Render `describeCell(world, pos)`'s prose + exit list as a read-only in-game panel (reuse the harness's proven output). No input parser (that's §POT-R1).

**Invariants:** ⚠️ **`ROOMS:CORE` (`ROOMS:CORE:START@9872`) is parity-fenced** — read its output, never edit the inlined copy; if `js/rooms.js` is touched, re-inline + `scripts/check-rooms-parity.js`. Render-only; Free-Movement N/A. **Verify:** reuse `tests/mud-harness.mjs` expectations for the rendered strings + eyeball (§7½). Sync `maps.md`/`docs/spec/spec-corridors.md`.

#### §HINT-01 — State-aware hints (needs a design call first) 🟡

**The finding (measured).** Quests carry a `hint:` field, **widely authored** (`grep -c "hint:"` = **1,057**), stating the stat + DC outright (e.g. *"See through the mockery — WIS Insight DC 11"*). Today's hint is a **constant string** — it doesn't know your inventory, level, or favor.

**The tension (why this needs a design call, not a loop task).** §POT-M2 wants a hint that knows *your* state — but this collides head-on with §PLAY-01's **honesty theme**: a hint that *hides* the DC to seem cleverer would be **less** honest, not more. The value depends entirely on which way it goes. **Do not build before the design call is made.**

**Increments:**
- [ ] **§HINT-01-0 — the design call (ASK).** Decide the shape: does a state-aware hint *add* context ("you already hold the Van Helsing route map") while **keeping** the DC visible (honesty-preserving), or does it replace the static string? Present the options; do not default. Only then scope A.

**Invariants:** TBD by the design call; if built, render-only. **Verify:** TBD. **This is an Ask-gate, not a loop item.**

#### §INNER-01 — A mechanic for the inner arc (the §POT-X2 design track; lab report first) 🟠

**The finding (measured).** Yael's monologue promises an inner arc outright — *"You came here to fight demons… **the ones behind your own eyes are older and meaner, and that is the real fight."*** There is **no mechanic for that fight**. The one shipped inner-arc mechanic is §GR-D's **Froberger Entry 42** (`entry42Written`/`entry42Text`/`entry42Read` (`entry42Written: false, entry42Text@22959`); `quest_ng_02` "The Open Page" (`quest_ng_02@10935`); carried across NG+) — the blank page you may fill at ≥3 dear friends, at the very end of the game. Entry 42 is **the proof the shape works and the template for more.**

**The design (a design call + lab report, NOT a loop task).** Extending the inner arc is a **new narrative arc** → per the Lab Report Policy it needs `lab-reports/lab-report-inner-arc.md` **locking data shapes before any HTML edit**, and it needs a **user design call** on what the mechanic *is* (a reflective journal like Entry 42, a fear/resolve track, moral weight — several are possible and they are not interchangeable). This row is a **placeholder for that design conversation**, not buildable inline.

**Increments:**
- [ ] **§INNER-01-0 — design call + lab report.** Decide the mechanic's shape (with the user); lock data shapes in a lab report; only then scope increments. Reuse Entry 42's cross-NG+ persistence pattern as the template.

**Invariants:** TBD by the lab report; likely render + a `_S_DEFAULTS()` field (declare once, per §STATE-INIT). **Verify:** TBD. **Design-call + lab-report gated.**

> **⟶ PRIOR (2026-08-03) — §DX-02l ✅ SHIPPED `91d3650`: the deliberate-backup endpoint got the `./api.sh` wrapper it never had — and the sweep the row asked for turned out to be the patch chain's input, deleted before anyone reads it.** On "continue" with §AUDIT-03d's staging question still unanswered, took the row the last board named as small. Existing-Work-First: tree clean at `bca468f`, server stopped, one other claude process, and the premise re-measured before any edit — `./api.sh save` answered `✗ Unknown command "save"`, `api/wb.js` held **0** occurrences of `save`/`snapshots`, and the *author-facing* guide reached the surface with `curl -s -XPOST …/api/save | jq` in **three** places, including §17.13 itself. So the golden rule was broken in the document that teaches it. **Shipped API-first — endpoint before wrapper (prompt.md §2.5):** `GET /api/snapshots` (dir, per-file size/mtime, total, and whether the patch chain already holds each one) and `DELETE /api/snapshots`, nonce-guarded like every other destructive route; then `./api.sh save` and `./api.sh snapshots [--sweep] [--force]`. `snapshot` joins the nonce endpoint's `validTypes` as **the one type with no collection behind it** — the sweep destroys files, not entries, so there is no key to resolve and the lookup is skipped rather than run against `undefined`. **The row's framing of the sweep is what failed on contact, and it failed in the safe direction for once.** It called disposal *"a `rm` a human has to remember"* — but `archive-snapshots.sh` already disposes of these files, and the way it does it is to diff each one into a `milepoints/patches` delta **and then `rm` it**. A blunt sweep is therefore not a chore automated; it is **the patch chain's input destroyed before it is read**, because an unarchived snapshot is the only copy of that state. `--sweep` now deletes only what the chain already holds (a `<stem>.patch`, or the name in `_last.name`), **names `./archive-snapshots.sh` in the skip reason**, and leaves `--force` as the deliberate discard. **Evidence:** a live round trip on the real server *before* any test — `./api.sh save` → 5.2 MB backup beside the game file → `snapshots` listed it `unarchived` → `--sweep` **kept it and said why** → `--sweep --force` freed it, ending with `roll2hit-v3.html` at its **original shasum** and the root back to one file; `dx02l-save-snapshots-cli.test.js` **7/7** against a throwaway server on a scratch copy (the sweep deletes files — it must never be pointed at the repo), and **all 7 fail on the stashed pre-fix tree** (restored, `shasum -c` OK — nothing here is vacuous); `check:walk` **15/15 exit 0**; full Playwright **764 passed / 4 failed** = the documented `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` four, **0 flaky**. **No HTML shipped and no §7½ — authoring surface only.** **The durable half is what the sweep is allowed to name:** a decoy suite (`roll2hit-v3-notes.html`, `-2026.html`, `-20260709.html`, `-20260709-0341.html`, `.htm`, `.html.bak`) survives a **forced** sweep sitting beside the game file, because only an exact `-YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS.html` stamp is reachable — that stamp is the entire guard between *delete the backups* and *delete the game*. The same file pins the row's own lesson as an assertion: **no author-facing doc may reach `/api/save` with `curl`.** **The lesson: an endpoint whose only documented client is `curl` is not a finished write path — the wrapper is what makes it real, and the gap is usually visible in the docs before it is visible in the CLI.** Docs: `api-user-guide.md` (§17.13 rewritten + both worked workflows + the endpoint card), `API-README.md` (new *Dated backups* section + 3 mapping rows), `wbapi-help.md`, `docs/notes/docs-dev-environment.md`, `index.md`, `prompt.md` §3 + §9, **CONTRIBUTING Hazard #7** (now carrying the reporting half — the directory is gitignored, so `./api.sh snapshots` is the only thing that will ever say what is in it), and `./api.sh help` gained a *Persistence* block under SERVER LIFECYCLE stating plainly that you do **not** need `save` after a write. **No side findings filed — the row was self-contained.** **Next: §AUDIT-03d (still the gating row — needs the staging design call; it releases 983 quests), else §DX-02j (measured, needs a per-group design call), §AUDIT-03o (measured, design call), §DX-01e-FU2 (design call), §AUDIT-03i/k/m, or §VM-01-G2b / the G4 child report.**

> **⟶ PRIOR (2026-08-03) — §DX-02k ✅ SHIPPED `2ead185`: the argless `save()` was not a stray test call. It was the server's own per-write path, and it wrote a 5.4 MB copy of the game into whatever directory the process started in, once per PUT, forever.** On "continue" with §AUDIT-03d's staging question still unanswered, took the row the last board named as *"the one I can just do."* Existing-Work-First: tree clean at `0abe376`, server stopped, one other claude process, and the premise re-measured before any edit. **It held, and then failed in two directions.** **(1) The destination is the process CWD, not "next to the module."** `getStampedName()` returns a **bare filename**, and `fs.writeFileSync` resolves a bare name against `process.cwd()` — proved by loading the repo's HTML from a scratch directory and watching the 5.4 MB snapshot land *in the scratch directory*. It read as "the repo root" only because that is where the server runs. **(2) The argless callers were not scripts — the SERVER was.** `saveAndRestart` and `saveAndVerify`, the persist step behind **every** successful PUT/POST/DELETE, ran `WBAPI.save()` argless and then `copyFileSync(snapshot, GAME_FILE)`, leaving the snapshot behind; two more argless sites sat in the batch endpoints. **So the row's own proposed fix — *"make the argless form server-only"* — would have left the actual litter source untouched**, which is the second time in two sessions that a §DX-02 row's framing survived contact and its *scope* did not. The snapshot was never a backup of the pre-write state either: **it holds the NEW text**, so it is pure redundancy the instant the copy lands. **The row's own caution came back inverted, too.** `archive-snapshots.sh` / `watch-snapshots.sh` / `monitor-snapshots.py` *do* depend on the stamped name — but `docs/notes/docs-dev-environment.md` documents their input as a **deliberate `cp`**. The per-write stamping was not feeding that feature, it was **drowning** it: a curated session history reduced to per-keystroke noise, patch chain last advanced **2026-07-09**, no watcher running, **6 orphans / ~32 MB** sitting in the root — invisible, because `roll2hit-v3-2*.html` is gitignored. **Shipped:** `save(dest)` writes exactly where told and **refuses with no argument**, naming the alternative in the error text so the next author cannot just pass `getStampedName()` back in; **`saveStamped([dir])`** is the deliberate dated backup and lands **beside the source file** (`POST /api/save`, `wbapi save`, and the audit-fix + junction-nuke endpoints, which carried the same bare-name CWD bug); **`saveGameFile()`** is the server-only per-write persist — temp beside the game file plus an **atomic rename**, which leaves nothing to sweep *and* means a reader can never catch a half-written game file, keeping the temp and returning its path if the rename fails. **Evidence:** the CWD claim proved by scratch-directory probe **before** any edit; **the pre-fix test run demonstrated the bug on itself** — stashing the fix and re-running dropped **two more 5.4 MB snapshots into the repo root**, the whole defect in one observation; `dx02k-save-destination.test.js` **6/6**, **5 failing on the stashed pre-fix tree** (restored, `shasum -c` OK — the passer is the `save(dest)` round trip, already true); live server round trip = **3 successful writes** (`act=1` idempotent, `act=2`, `act=1` back), each confirmed on disk by grep and through `GET`, ending at the **original shasum** with **0 new snapshots and 0 temp files**; `POST /api/save` still returns a dated backup beside the game file, so the archive surface is intact; `check:walk` **15/15 exit 0**; full Playwright **756 passed / 4 failed** = the documented crud-arrays four (+6 new tests over the 750 baseline). **One honest wrinkle:** the first full run showed **5** failed — `multiplayer-presence:171` red on *both* attempts, where the baseline calls it a flake that retries green. It is 7/7 alone and a **second full run reproduced the documented baseline exactly** (4 failed / 1 flaky / 756 passed), so it is the known flake having a bad run, not a regression; the file spawns its own throwaway server and performs no writes. Root swept to **one** `roll2hit-v3*.html`, and two full suite runs added none back. **The durable half is the inverse assertion in the test: no shipped code may call `WBAPI.save()` with no destination** — the bug is invisible in behaviour (the write succeeds, the game is correct) and findable only by looking at a directory nobody named. **The lesson, and the natural sequel to §DX-02h's: "did it persist?" is only half of a write path's acceptance test — the other half is "and nowhere else."** Docs: **CONTRIBUTING Hazard #7**, `index.md` (Version Snapshots row + `wbapi-core.js` persist surfaces), `docs/notes/docs-dev-environment.md`, `docs/api/api-user-guide.md` (which told authors `POST /api/save` was how a write reached disk — it already had), plus annotations on the three historical notes still tracing the old stamped path. **Side finding filed, not swept: §DX-02l** — `POST /api/save` has **no `./api.sh` wrapper**, so verifying it needed raw `curl`, the inverse of prompt.md §3's rule; worth pairing with an `./api.sh snapshots` list/sweep now that snapshots are on-request only. **Next: §AUDIT-03d (still the gating row — needs the staging design call; it releases 983 quests), else §DX-02j (measured, needs a per-group design call), §AUDIT-03o (measured, design call), §DX-01e-FU2 (design call), §DX-02l (small), §AUDIT-03i/k/m, or §VM-01-G2b / the G4 child report.**

> **⟶ PRIOR (2026-08-03) — §DX-02h + §DX-02i ✅ SHIPPED TOGETHER `a047348`: the row said one monster was unreachable; 55 are. The doc that made it look rostered described a function that does not exist. And the endpoint for fixing it reported success while writing nothing — with GET agreeing.** On "continue" with §AUDIT-03d's design question still unanswered, took the two rows the last board named as small. Existing-Work-First: tree clean at `5cf04d4`, server stopped, one other claude process, and grep confirmed **both** premises before anything was written. **Both framings then failed, in the direction this repo keeps finding.** Scanning all 398 `MONSTER_POOL` keys for a reference outside `MONSTER_POOL`/`MONSTER_DROPS`, **55 have none** — the row's `void_rat_swarm` was not unique, its `voidTainted` twin **`void_wolf`** had the identical 2-reference shape, and ~50 more sit in the *partially* rostered Dark Fantasy Bestiary (`fleder` orphaned, `protofleder` live). **Why nobody noticed is the sharp part:** `world.md` stated in the present tense that both were *"injected into terrain encounter tables at runtime when `voidPressure ≥ 6` via `_applyVoidPressureMonsters()`"* — that function occurs **0 times in the file**, and **2 of the 5 terrains it named (`dark_forest`, `mountain_pass`) have never been keys**. The doc was the thing that looked like evidence (§AUDIT-03l's class), which is exactly why *re-measure, never read placement off a doc* is not optional. **The fix was then blocked by a third defect on its own path:** `PUT /api/terrain` accepted only `{label,icon}` and **persisted nothing** — it set the in-memory model, returned `ok:true` with the entity echoed, never called `save()`, and because the mutation lived in the loaded model **`GET` read the phantom edit back for the rest of the process's life**. That is worse than the DELETE family it mirrors (§DX-01d/i), where the entry at least reappeared on the next parse: here the read path *corroborated* the lie. Root cause was structural — `editField`/`editStructuredField` carried a `sectionMap` with **no `terrain` entry**, so `WORLD_DB` had no source-level writer at all, and the server's own advisory had been telling authors to `PUT {"monsters":[…]}`, an operation the endpoint rejected. **Fixed API-first (endpoint before edit, prompt.md §2.5), then authored through it in one call.** The roster got its **own** writer rather than reusing `editStructuredField`, because `WORLD_DB` is the one collection whose array holds **code identifiers** (`P.giant_rat`): the generic path would emit `["giant_rat"]`, which re-parses to strings with nothing throwing — `_buildIndexes` maps them fine — while the GAME reads those entries as stat blocks, so `_monsterLevel` would score the whole terrain 1. It re-parses the patched section and **proves the roster reads back before committing**, and `editField` now refuses `monsters` outright (insertStringField would otherwise add a *second* `monsters:` key — §AUDIT-03a's rot). **A live detail decided the validation:** `cat_quarter` ships a duplicate (`fluffy_cat` ×2), so duplicates warn instead of failing — a writer that cannot express what the corpus already holds cannot round-trip it. **§DX-02i** deleted `XP_BY_TIER` (user chose delete over annotate) after measuring it at **1.9×–6.5× below** the shipped `(S.enemy.ac||10) * (S.opp.maxHp||10)` at every tier — `deadly` a flat 1000 against a mean of **4,095** — so it was a different model, not a stale form of the shipped one; four dependents had to move, one of them `index.md`'s row whose own text (*"L12+ uses AC × maxHP"*) was also false. **Evidence:** the terrain defect proved by round trip **first** (`ok:true` + `GET` agreeing + 0 occurrences on disk); placement `git diff` = **1 line**; **golden parse diff across all 7 collections = 1 entry diff, `sewers fields=monsters`**, 0 lost / 0 gained; HTTP negative controls refused with the file **byte-identical by shasum**; `dx02h-terrain-roster-write.test.js` **6/6** with **5 failing on the stashed pre-fix tree** (restored, `shasum -c` OK — the passer is the inverse guard, already true); `check:walk` **15/15 exit 0**; full Playwright **750 passed / 4 failed** = the documented crud-arrays four. **§7½ DONE:** the pre-battle overlay at SFT now renders **VOID RAT SWARM · Visby Sewers** with a styled green **EASY** badge, and `_weightedMonsterPick('sewers')` drew the swarm 172 / the wolf 241 times in 4,000; 0 page errors. **The durable half is the inverse assertion** — every terrain roster entry must parse to a **stat block, not a bare key string** — because a JSON-string roster re-parses without error and is invisible to every other gate. **The lesson, now proved in three directions (create §DX-01c, delete §DX-01d/i, update §DX-02h): a write path's acceptance test is a ROUND TRIP — and "GET agrees with me" is not that test.** Docs: CONTRIBUTING **Hazard #6**, `monsters.md` §"A pool entry is not content until a roster names it", `world.md` drift-corrected, both API docs (which documented adding monsters via a PUT that could not do it, in raw `curl`), `index.md`. **Side findings filed, not swept: §DX-02j** (the **53** remaining unreachable pool entries — decide per group, then a `check:monsterreach` gate in the §AUDIT-03j/n mould) and **§DX-02k** (argless `WBAPI.save()` stamps a snapshot into the repo root; two are sitting there now from earlier sessions, hidden by gitignore). **Next: §AUDIT-03d (still the gating row — needs the staging design call; it releases 983 quests), else §DX-02j (measured, needs a per-group design call), §AUDIT-03o (measured, design call), §DX-01e-FU2 (design call), §DX-02k (small), §AUDIT-03i/k/m, or §VM-01-G2b / the G4 child report.**

> **⟶ PRIOR (2026-07-30) — §DX-01d + §DX-01i ✅ SHIPPED TOGETHER: every `del` in the API reported success and changed nothing on disk. Not just `del monster`, not just junctions — the whole DELETE family, for every key.** On "continue" with §AUDIT-03d's design question still unanswered, took the pairing the last board named first (*"the two `del` defects are one class — cheaper paired"*). Existing-Work-First: tree clean at `e4c03a0`, server stopped, a 2nd claude process present but the game file untouched. **Measured before editing, in-process against a COPY** (`roll2hit-v3.html` never written): `monster:fish_01`, `quest:mq_1` and `node:RUH` each returned `ok:true`, each left `_rawSrc` **byte-identical**, and each **came back after save+reload**. Both rows described the symptom correctly and **understated the scope by 3×** — every `delete()` in `wbapi-core.js` was the single line `delete WBAPI.<collection>[key]`, and the server's generic `DELETE` handler ended at `json(...)` with **no `saveAndVerify` at all**, so nothing reached disk even in principle. The precedent §DX-01i pointed at (`the node-delete splice at wbapi-core.js ~1260`) was **dead code with zero callers**, and its private brace-walker missed `/* */` comments and orphaned the `NODE_COORDS` row besides. **This is §DX-01c in mirror image — a write path that reports success without persisting — and the lesson is the same in both directions: nothing throws, so the failure is silent.** **Shipped:** `WBAPI.deleteEntrySource(section,key)`, one source-level excision for all four types with **verify-or-revert** (the section's depth-1 key *multiset* must lose exactly one instance of `key`, or the splice is rolled back and the delete fails loudly rather than corrupting the section) and clamped past nested START anchors so a pool delete can never reach the trophy-drops map; **the `check:dupkeys` scanner moved into `wbapi-core.js`** and imported back by the gate, so audit and writer share ONE scanner (two would drift — the §AUDIT-03f class); **cascades** (node→`NODE_COORDS`, monster→`MONSTER_DROPS`) so no orphan is left for `./api.sh audit`; the server's DELETE now routes through `saveAndVerify` with a new **`postVerify`** hook — a delete's proof is an *absence* in the re-parsed collections, which `expectedFields` cannot express — returning `deleteVerified:true`; and **`highway --execute` deprecated + refused up front** (before the coordinate fetch, so it needs no server) with the real recipe in the error. **Evidence:** `dx01di-delete-persists.test.js` **8/8**, including **the 20 largest nested UQF bodies excised one at a time** (largest 3,350 bytes of braces/strings/arrow-fns, each costing exactly one entry); **end-to-end through the live server twice, each a byte-identical no-op** — `post monster dx01i_probe` → `del monster` → `grep -c` **0** (the row's own reproduction, which used to leave **1**), and `post node ZZP` → `del node` → **0**, game file `shasum 018ee943…` unchanged both times; `check:walk` **12/12 exit 0**; full Playwright **717 passed / 4 failed** — the baseline is exactly the documented four in `worldbuilder-crud-arrays.test.js` plus the known `multiplayer-presence` flake (passed on retry). **No HTML shipped and no §7½ — authoring-surface only; nothing a player sees changed.** **Docs:** a new **CONTRIBUTING Hazard #5** carries the class forward (*whenever you add a write path, the acceptance test is a round trip — save, re-parse, assert the change survived*), Hazards #2/#3/#4 updated to ✅, prompt.md §3 #4/#5, `api-user-guide.md` §10, `API-README.md`, and `./api.sh help` (§21 no longer advertises **eleven** `highway --execute` invocations that now die). **Next: §AUDIT-03d (still the gating row — needs the staging design call; 3 bare `--retries=0` runs went **fail / pass / fail** (DEX 8 vs DC 13 → `d20+1`, a ~45% coin flip — the "reproduces on a bare re-run" reading came from the 2-retry config). `warrants-board:999` was subtler: **every standing assertion already passed** (ESCALATED `+4`, idempotent, `questDone`) and only `posAfter ≠ posBefore` failed, because the test hand-set `playerR/C = 5,9` alongside `currentCode = 'RVP'` — a state no step can produce — so `storyRender`'s §CELL-03 sync (`31618`) corrected the coords to RVP's real cell (17,180) and the test read the correction as a warp. **No jump travel, no player move.** **Shipped (test-only, `roll2hit-v3.html` untouched):** `effort-xp` forces the fail through the engine's own dial via a scoped `forceFail(id, fn)` that raises `sc.dc` to 999 and restores in `finally` — the same idiom `warrants-board` already used for a forced PASS (`sc.dc = -100`); `warrants-board` derives its stand-on cell from `NODE_COORDS[q.activateNode]`. **Evidence:** both suites **28/28 on three consecutive `--retries=0` runs** (no flake masking); full Playwright **710 passed / 4 failed** (the documented crud-arrays four); ### §VM-01 — The Quest VM → *No Word for Wait* (NEW 2026-07-16)

> **Diagnostic (parent) lab report:** `lab-reports/lab-report-javascript-mud.md` — a structural read of the entire `<script>` block (five layers, four execution traces: program/UI/movement/quest-acceptance), plus the follow-up verification recorded below. **Not** a quest-by-quest audit. **Inc A child lab report LOCKED 2026-07-21:** `lab-reports/lab-report-vm01a-execbits-coroutine.md` (design-review-before-implementation; locks the `execBits`→generator transformation, the `ask` envelope, the driver, the five call-site wraps, the three design calls, and the test plan — see §RESUME NEWEST banner above). Policy: **Host/Script Separation Policy** in [CONTRIBUTING.md](CONTRIBUTING.md).
>
> **The unifying theme (matched to `story.md`): the engine tells, and cannot ask.** `story.md` is about a man who saw the Void and never managed to *ask anyone for help* — Froberger fixed it alone and it destroyed him. The quest engine commits the same act on the player: **you are told (`📋 <title>`), never asked.** Arrival + gate = `'active'`, announced in a `·`-joined message strip. There is no accept button — and the reason is not that nobody added one. **There is no state in which the VM is waiting for an answer.** (§PLAY-01 found the engine *knows what it won't transmit*; §VM-01 finds it **cannot receive**. Same sin, opposite direction.)
>
> **The architecture is already right — this track finishes §ARCH-01, it does not redesign it.** The file runs a proper embedded-scripting split twice over: three pure, world-injected, parity-fenced kernels (`MOVER`/`ROOMS`/`DUEL`, byte-identical to `js/*.js` with CI assertions), and a 258-line `QuestRuntime` executing 11,106 lines of `QUEST_DB` through the `BIT_CONTRACTS` opcode table. That 43:1 ratio is the achievement. **The defect is one small thing on top of it.**
>
> **Root cause — the VM has twelve opcodes and no jump instruction.** `execBits` (`execBits(bits, ctx)@22081`) in full: a `for` loop that looks up `HANDLERS[bit.kind]`, calls `h(bit, ctx)`, and ignores the return. No branch. No suspend. Three verified proofs, all grepped this session, none recalled:
>
> 1. **`choice` has a complete contract and an empty handler.** The contract (`choice:      { required@21860`) is rigorous — requires `prompt` + `options`, validates ≥2 options each carrying a `label` and a `bits` array. The implementation (`choice(bit@22154`) is, in full: `choice(bit, ctx) { /* Phase 2: renderChoiceBlock(bit.prompt, bit.options, ctx) */ }`. **Zero quests use it** (`grep -c "kind:'choice'"` → 0). It was never written because **a synchronous `for` loop cannot wait for a human being.**
> 2. **`item_check` writes a predicate nobody reads.** `ctx._itemCheck@22146` sets it; a full-file grep returns that line and nothing else. It is a conditional in a language with no `if` — it evaluates and evaporates.
> 3. **`skill_check` works only by smuggling.** It is the one bit that branches, and it does so by hardcoding the branch *inside the leaf*: `resolveSkillCheck` → `execBits(pass ? bit.onPass : bit.onFail)` (`execBits(pass ?@22116`). The sole conditional in the whole language is baked into one instruction rather than available to it.
>
> **Two missing words, and everything follows.** `wait` (no `yield` → no consent, no `choice`, no dialogue tree, no multi-step interaction, no `'abandoned'` status, no transactional bit chain — a throw mid-chain leaves half the effects applied with no rollback). `or` (no boolean nesting → each new OR shape has historically meant a new *term*; `itemsMinAny` was added for exactly one quest, `quest_wm_01`). **Prior art worth reading before Inc A:** Ink (Inkle) compiles narrative to JSON bytecode on a small VM and suspends at a `ChoicePoint` — structurally what this is; Yarn Spinner for dialogue; CEL / JSONLogic for Inc F (expression-AST-as-data, no parser needed).
>
> **Per Lab Report Policy: every increment locks its own child lab report (data shapes + flow) before any HTML edit.** The rows below are the *plan of a plan* — scope, evidence, dependency, and the decision each one carries. They are not implementation plans and must not be treated as one.
>
> **Dependency order:** A · B · C are independent (A is the keystone). D needs C. E needs C + the `_legacy_fn` purge. F is independent but cheaper after C. G needs A + F to be worth doing.

> *Closed increments A–F + F-FU (A/B/C `c22f4f0` · D `9f10bfe` · E `354b20a` · F `c6be7f8` · F-FU `549d6b4`) — full ship records moved verbatim → plan-archive.md §"Archived 2026-07-28 — §DX-01f". Only §VM-01-G below remains open.*

- [~] **§VM-01-G — *The migration front*: per-node special cases → node hooks — DESIGN LOCKED 2026-07-28 (`lab-reports/lab-report-vm01g-migration-front.md`): user chose Option C (layered) via Ask — NODE_PANELS ordered data table for the ~30 Class-A/B flavor/arrival blocks · NODE_HOOKS registered render fns for the ~9 Class-E bespoke UIs · Class C/D migrate INTO the VM per-arc (real `gate:`+`activateNode`; `choice` + a new `cost` leaf — Inc A's first content consumer); slice order G1→G2→G3→G4 confirmed; G4 OK in principle but needs its own child design pass first. Re-measure at lock: front at 33332, `node.code` comparisons 76→124 (77 above the front across ~58 blocks + 47 engine-region Class-F, logged as G-FU) — the front moves backward ~4/day, the row's thesis is measurable, not theoretical.** Original finding: `storyRender` (now `30887`–`35298`, **4,412 lines — the largest function in the file**) is two programs stacked: ~2,445 lines of hardcoded per-node special cases followed by a compact, generic, data-driven `_mkSection`/`_mkCard` engine that renders Fish/Encounter/Quests/Loot/Rest/World from data. Every `if (node.code === 'XX')` above the front is a missing component — `isFishingLake` is what one looks like when it went right. This is plain ECS, **the same disease as the gate language one layer up:** *when the data language lacks a shape, the file grows imperative code instead of vocabulary.* Two hazards the report names as load-bearing: **insertion order is LIFO** (every panel block inserts `afterend` of `story-text-box`, so later blocks render ABOVE earlier — the table must be order-preserving by construction) and **remove-then-recreate semantics** (render is re-entrant; remove managed ids first, then create the active set). The §PLAY-01-G lesson applies directly — **`birkaNpcs` had five NPC cards keyed to dead pre-§WALK node codes and rendered nowhere for months**; special-case blocks keyed on `node.code` rot silently. Slices: **G1** NODE_PANELS (~700–900 lines → one table + ~40-line renderer; provable no-op via golden-DOM parity) · **G2** NODE_HOOKS (verbatim extraction, golden-DOM parity) · **G3** Class C per-arc (WM/cat/VS/NG+ CI → real gates; arc tests + `check:questgraph`) · **G4** Class D per-verb (child report first) · **G-FU** the 47 engine-region exceptions. **⟶ G1 ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-28 (`25c3710` + fixes `bf6f486`/`7a130cd`):** 12 order-safe Class-A/B panels (CI tagline · AO name-change · KS conversion · LT stoning · §SIREN teaser · LCA close · Malta snake · Sweelinck variant · Quest −1 door · IST/TBS/MCT approaches) migrated into the ordered `NODE_PANELS` table + one `_renderNodePanels` loop at the stale-sibling sweep; once-flags (`saulConverted`/`maltaSnakeEvent`) via the table's `once` field; LIFO stacking replicated by construction (table order = old source order, all inserts afterend of `#story-text-box`). **Proof: 24-combo golden-DOM before/after diff = byte-identical** (DOM + flag writes; temp harness deleted after the proof), `uqf-node-panels.test.js` 7/7 permanent regression, `quest-runtime-uqf` **303/303**, `npc-card-map` 23/23, all `check:walk` sub-checks green (QUEST:CORE 22519 bytes identical), only J14/J15 + TGS/SPB baseline reds. **Implementation refinements over the lab report (grep-before-building):** (1) the report's ~22-block Class-A count included the Birka-region sub-blocks, which anchor to `npcRowDiv` not `story-text-box` — a different anchor class, excluded from G1 (candidates for a second table after G2); (2) order-safety excludes the BK approach panel (Corelli's button precedes it and can co-fire) and the NUE/TLS mid-sequence reminders (Prior Carrier/Inquisitor/Codex-Core) — these stay inline until G3/G4 migrate the conflicting Class-C/D neighbors, which dissolves the constraint. **G1 findings — two DEAD panels surfaced and fixed as one-line follow-ups:** the §SIREN-01 navigator teaser was keyed to `LJ3`, a node code that was NEVER BUILT (maps.md documents LJ0–LJ3 sea-battle junctions; only the courts shipped) → remapped to LSO, `quest_sea_overseer`'s activateNode (`bf6f486`); the §LXII AO name-change notice used `!visited['HTY']` as its once-guard, but `storyCollectLoot` flips `visited[code]` earlier in the same render, dead since it shipped → dedicated `aoNameNoticeSeen` once-flag (`7a130cd`). Both now render for the first time ever — the row's silent-rot thesis, proven twice more by the migration itself. Docs synced: story.md §SIREN injections + maps.md junction rows flagged ⚠ NOT AS-BUILT (the section's plan topology/coords diverge from NODE_MAP — a candidate for a future maps.md sync pass). **⟶ G2 ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-28: NODE_HOOKS — 7 Class-E bespoke blocks (~440 lines) leave storyRender as verbatim registered hooks, proven byte-identical.** Extracted: Void Archaeology (Layer 52, 5-node investigate UI) · Void Shaman Warden (Layer 56) · Corelli Merchant (Layer 61) · Codex Core Chamber (§D01-10) · La Riva row (Layer 78) · Scholar Workshop (Layer 81) · Mimic Meadows (Layer 81) — each hook body is the VERBATIM former block, still self-gating. **One design deviation, recorded in the lab report §8:** the sketched "single dispatch loop" is order-UNSAFE — Corelli's gate is the computed `_checkCorelliAppearance` schedule (any of 5 nodes), so no single dispatch position avoids crossing a same-node co-firing block, and a keyed `NODE_HOOKS[code]` map can't even express that gate. Shipped shape = ordered `NODE_HOOKS` registry (`nodes` = tooling metadata, `null` for computed schedules) + **in-place dispatch**: `_runNodeHook(id, node)` sits at the exact source position each block occupied, so LIFO stacking is preserved **by construction** — the G1 order-safety rule needs no per-block exclusion analysis, and G2b/G3 candidates aren't order-blocked. **Proof: 14-combo golden-DOM before/after diff byte-identical** (story-column HTML + move-msg + quest/flag probes, 0 page errors both sides; temp harness deleted); new `uqf-node-hooks.test.js` **4/4** (registry integrity · one live surface probe per hook · LIFO pin vs the inline ZRH Secret Gate · source guard: no block body remains inline); regression `quest-runtime-uqf` **303/303** + `uqf-node-panels` 7/7 + `npc-card-map` 22/22 (332/332 in one run); all four parity fences byte-identical (`MOVER` 1847 / `ROOMS` 10836 / `QUEST` 22519 / `DUEL` 7987), `check:gateast` 76 / `check:rng` / `check:questgraph` green; only the documented J14/J15 + TGS/SPB baseline reds. Side-finding: fresh SZG legitimately renders no workshop panel (the node's own `loot` auto-grants the Prototype Wand on arrival, gating the wand button off) — test probe adjusted, not a regression. **Deferred to G2b:** the Birka-region nested UIs (Blue Shutters · Froberger Memorial · Pachelbel/Couperin ledgers · Pit Championship · Kenickie shop · Kern & Sable) — `npcRowDiv` anchor / Layer-41 scope, need a ctx-argument design; CDG/Tilbury/Visby mixed blocks are G3's territory. Hazard #1 respected (`:1367` verified stopped). **Next slice: G3 (Class C per-arc quest activation → real gates), or G2b.** `storyRender` (`30502`–`34862`, **4,360 lines — the largest function in the file**) is two programs stacked: ~2,400 lines of hardcoded per-node special cases (76 `node.code === '…'` comparisons across ~40 blocks; Birka NPC cards alone are `31810`–`32917`) followed at `32918` by a compact, generic, data-driven `_mkSection`/`_mkCard` engine that renders Fish/Encounter/Quests/Loot/Rest/World from data. **The migration front is a horizontal line at 32918 and the open question is whether it's still moving.** Every `if (node.code === 'XX')` above it is a missing component — `isFishingLake` is what one looks like when it went right (add the flag in `NODE_MAP`, the node gets a fishing card, ~8 lines). This is plain ECS. **It is the same disease as the gate language one layer up:** *when the data language lacks a shape, the file grows imperative code instead of vocabulary.* **Which is why it is last** — fix the vocabulary in A and F first and a real fraction of the 2,400 lines becomes expressible as data rather than needing a bespoke hook. **Lock first:** whether node hooks are `[bits]` (reuse the VM — attractive once A lands) or registered render fns; and the §PLAY-01-G lesson applies directly — **`birkaNpcs` had five NPC cards keyed to dead pre-§WALK node codes and rendered nowhere for months.** Special-case blocks keyed on `node.code` rot silently. That is the argument for this increment, and it is not theoretical. **G3 ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-28 — Class C is done, and the silent-rot thesis proved out three more times over** (full record: lab report §9). The 5 activation blocks (NG+/WM/TL/VS/cat — 15 quests + vs_02/vs_03/vs_warden/la_riva_02/03 adjacent) became real `gate:`+`activateNode` data with two new host-understood fields — **`onActivate`** (absent → `📋 title` · null → silent · `{msg,delayMs}` → the arcs' bespoke staged narrations, verbatim) and **`boardExempt`** (NG+ remembrance never posts) — and the activation loop was extracted to `_uqfActivateAtNode`, run at storyRender START so per-node UI keyed on `'active'` sees same-arrival activations (the Solvak-button lesson). **Zero new gate leaves needed** (`countMin` on `ngPlusRun`, `questsDone`, `notFlags` covered everything — the "act ≥ N" legs were structurally dead: `actNumber` = `node.act`, constant per node). **What grep-before-building found:** (1) audit waves `a721254`/`ea02faf` had APPENDED duplicate `activateNode` keys below the authored `activateNode:null` — last-key-wins had the whole cat chain + tl_02/03 + vs_02/03/warden + la_riva_02/03 mass-activating with vacuous gates, so the legacy stanzas' staging was already dead; (2) `710bb75`'s SF→LCY "fix" was a wrong-target dead-code remap (SF = Storefront = STN) — tl_01/tl_03 pointed at the wrong city; (3) **`NODE_MAP.VS` and `NODE_MAP.TL` had no `code` field — the entire VS arc and the TL Vonn path had NEVER run in live play**; fixed via `./api.sh put node` (+ two latent double-pay bugs in the revived Ori/seal buttons fixed by paying through the quests' own onComplete chains, keyed on quest-active). Board consequence (deliberate): `_bountyPostable`'s "gate satisfied ⇒ in-sequence" promise is now TRUE — the slate sheds exactly the out-of-sequence chapters; FU6 legitimacy tests updated to in-sequence-at-referral-time; Yva's button re-keyed on `quest_vs_02` active heals the referral dead-end. **Evidence:** 20-combo pre/post golden-state diff **14 byte-identical · 6 intended · 0 unexpected**; new `uqf-quest-activation.test.js` **8/8**; `quest-runtime-uqf` 303/303, warrants-board 25/25, npc-card-map 22/22, panels 7 + hooks 4 + gate-ast/coroutine/env/softlock/quest-core + 4 smokes (410 total in final runs); all four parity fences byte-identical (QUEST:CORE untouched); `check:walk` green except the documented J14/J15 + TGS/SPB baselines; §7½ eyeballed (VS: Solvak card + button + listed quest; CDG: staged). Docs synced: quest.md (4 new arc tables + cat activation model), world.md (§XIX/§XX PLANNED→LIVE + revival notes), prompt.md §4 (`onActivate`/`boardExempt`), lab report §9. **Next: G2b (npcRowDiv ctx design) or the G4 child report.**

### §DX-01 — Developer-Experience & Repo-Health refactors (NEW 2026-07-22)

> **What this is.** A curated batch of *large refactor-style suggestions and side tangents* surfaced while writing **`prompt.md`** (the operating directive) and re-reading the full project on 2026-07-22. **Every claim below was grep-verified against the live repo this session** — line/count/red figures are measured, not recalled (grep-before-building). **None of these were built** — they are logged so the main task (prompt.md + README/index sync) could finish without turning a tangent into a detour, exactly the finishing discipline `prompt.md §2.9` prescribes. Each is its own increment; **each still needs the normal gate** (lab report only if it grows to a redesign; API-first; doc sync; verify). Ordered by payoff-to-risk.

- [x] **§DX-01a — `check:walk` fully green ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-28 (also closes §JUNK-01).** Both 20-day baseline reds retired; **no checker was taught an allowlist — the data was genuinely wrong, both ways, and both reds were the SAME defect.** Reproduced live first (identical to the row's figures), then: **(1) J14/J15 removed** — hand-edit per Hazard #4 with the server confirmed stopped (Hazard #1): the two `junction:true` NODE_MAP entries + NODE_COORDS rows + TGS's dangling `S:"J14"` deleted; repo-wide grep proved zero other live references (maps.md's "J14" is the *historical* Malta-approach node 104, a different entity; milepoints/ is a snapshot). **(2) Key discovery: R2's three off-component "settlements" 7,217/8,217/9,217 were exactly TGS+J14+J15** — the §KG highway tool dropped the stubs and never laid the road its own signpost promised ("The Tungas–Station 7 (Dark) Road"; SVO sits at 14,217, straight south). **(3) `build-roads.js --apply` regen** (the block's own "do not hand-edit" path; config/roads-pins.json untouched, unrelated region): cell-diff vs HEAD = **394/411 unchanged**, and every change is one of three intended stories — the real Tungas–Station 7 road laid (col 217 rows 8–13, TGS↔SVO), the TLL→SPB corridor rerouted off Gulf-of-Finland sea cell 10,207 (R3) via the row-8 TUO-stack corridor, and a local southern re-carve. **Evidence:** `check:walk` **all 10 gates green** — I1/I2/I3 (416/416 reachable, 1 component), R1–R4 (244/244 settlements, 0 overlaps, 410 cells 1.5%), all four parity fences + gateast 76 + rng + questgraph (0 prober-gaps, 0 nondeterminism); `quest-runtime-uqf` **303/303** (also proves the inline `<script>` parses). Docs synced: prompt.md §7 baseline-reds → **"NONE — any red is a real regression"**; index.md counts 418→**416** nodes (+ stats-refreshed lines/size, `npm run stats`). §DX-01d (deprecating the tool that created the stubs) stays open — scope-fenced, no server code touched.

> *§DX-01b ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-22 `bd951d7` — the "~17–18-failure env baseline" was disproved (17 deterministic stale tests, no flake); 16 fixed, 17 → 1, the git-stash-diff ceremony retired; the 1 remaining red became §BOARD-01-VOID-GATE (since shipped). Full record: plan-archive.md §"Archived 2026-07-28 — §DX-01f".*

- [x] **§DX-01c — `./api.sh post monster` FIXED ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-30 `dca81ae`. WBAPI Hazard #2 is retired, and with it the API-first exception that made all 398 monsters hand-authored.** The clean fix was in scope, so the "make it fail loudly" fallback applied only to *bad input*. **The row's premise held exactly, and the root cause was one the row hadn't named: `MONSTER_POOL` is the only section in the file that NESTS another.** `MONSTER_DROPS:START/END` (5796/6269) sits *inside* `MONSTER_POOL:START/END` (5354/6270), so `insertBeforeSectionClose`'s `lastIndexOf('\n};', endAnchor)` resolved to the **trophy-drops map's** closing brace. Every created monster was spliced into a real-but-wrong object — **which is why it never threw.** Stacked on that, `serializeMonsterLiteral` wrote a schema the game does not have: `{name, ac, hp, atk, dmg, xp, tier}` — no `key`, no `dmgDie/dmgCount/dmgFlat` (so the monster had *no damage at all*), and the string tier through `Number()` → `tier:NaN`. **Shipped:** (1) `WBAPI._sectionCloseIdx` in `wbapi-core.js` — stops the search at the first nested START anchor; `insertBeforeSectionClose` now delegates, so all 12 create call-sites are covered and the class can't recur if another section is ever wrapped. (2) `WBAPI.monsters.create/validate/serialize` — the live 9-field contract, measured (398/398 entries carry exactly `{key,name,ac,hp,atk,dmgDie,dmgCount,dmgFlat,tier}`, plus `voidTainted` on 2); `key` is always emitted (a keyless entry is the §AUDIT-03e shared-slot class). (3) `serializeMonsterLiteral` **deleted**; the POST route delegates and returns **422 + the offending field list + a worked example, writing nothing**, for an incomplete body, a numeric tier, or the retired `dmg`/`xp`. (4) Seven server help/`howToFix` curls that *taught the broken body* corrected, plus `tier=boss` → `deadly` in `api-user-guide.md` (there is no `boss` tier — the example would now 422). **Verified end-to-end against the running server, not just in unit:** `./api.sh post monster key=dock_rat …` → git diff = **exactly one line, inside MONSTER_POOL, before its own `};`**; the old-style body (`dmg=3 xp=10 tier=1`) → 422 listing all six defects; game file restored to its original md5 (`d5afa4a3…`) afterward. `tests/integration/dx01c-monster-create.test.js` **7/7** — including a test that **pins the trap** (a naive last-`};`-before-END must still resolve *inside* MONSTER_DROPS, so a revert fails here rather than in the trophy map) and one asserting the six non-nesting sections are unaffected. `check:walk` **12/12 green**; full Playwright **708 passed / 6 failed, all 6 proved pre-existing at HEAD by stash-diff** (the 4 documented `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` + 2 previously-undocumented — see §DX-02f). **Docs:** CONTRIBUTING.md Hazard #2 rewritten as a working recipe that keeps the defect class as the lesson (*a write that lands in a real-but-wrong object never throws*), prompt.md §3 hazard #2, potential.md, `monsters.md` gains an Authoring section, `api-user-guide.md` §9.3. **Side-findings filed, not detoured: §DX-01i** (`del monster` is model-only) **and §DX-02g** (2 off-contract tiers).

- [x] **§DX-01i — `del monster` is model-only ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-30 `2f467f3` (jointly with §DX-01d — see that row for the full record).** The row's premise held, and **the scope was three times bigger than it named**: measuring it on a copy of the shipped file showed `monster`, `quest` **and** `node` all returning `ok:true`, all leaving `_rawSrc` byte-identical, all three surviving save+reload — the whole DELETE family, for every key, not just monsters and not just junctions. The row's suggested precedent (`the node-delete splice at wbapi-core.js ~1260`) turned out to be **dead code nothing ever called**. Original row text preserved in the §DX-01d record below.

- [x] **§DX-02g — the two off-contract tiers ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-31 `f229ede`. The row called it an authoring-surface defect. It was four live gameplay surfaces, and every one of them degraded SILENTLY.** The premise held exactly — `void_shaman:'rare'`, `void_rat_swarm:'low'`, the other 396 on contract — but the framing (*"this is an authoring-surface defect, not a gameplay one"*) was wrong, and measuring it before editing is what caught that. **`tier` is read in six places and every reader FALLS BACK rather than throwing:** `_voidEnrage(tier)` → `|| {atk:1,dmg:1,die:0}`, and The Warden matches `_VOID_ENEMY_RE`, so its §PLAY-01-B low-HP press ran at the **weakest magnitude in the game** (+1/+1) instead of hard's +3/+3 — on a sealed Act-5 guardian with a bespoke 4-item weighted drop table; The doc was the thing that looked like evidence (§AUDIT-03l's class). **The row's own fix was blocked by a third defect: `PUT /api/terrain` accepted only `{label, icon}` — and persisted NOTHING.** It set `WBAPI.worldDb[k][f]` in memory, returned `ok:true` with the entity echoed, and never called `save()`; **`GET` then read the phantom edit back for the rest of the process's life**, so unlike the DELETE family (§DX-01d/i) the read path *corroborated* the lie. Root cause: `editField`/`editStructuredField` carried a `sectionMap` with **no `terrain` entry**, so `WORLD_DB` had no source-level writer at all — and the server's own advisory already told authors to `PUT {"monsters":[…]}`, an operation the endpoint rejected. **Shipped, API-first (endpoint before edit, prompt.md §2.5):** `terrain` added to the section map; a dedicated **`WBAPI.editTerrainRoster(key, monsterKeys)`**, because `WORLD_DB` is the one collection whose array holds **code identifiers** (`P.giant_rat`) — `editStructuredField` would emit `["giant_rat"]`, which re-parses to strings and silently drives `_monsterLevel` to 1 for the whole terrain (§DX-01c's real-but-wrong-object, third dimension); it re-parses the patched section and **proves the roster reads back before committing** (`deleteEntrySource`'s refuse-rather-than-corrupt), the server re-verifies after the disk reload, and `editField` now **refuses** `monsters` outright (patchStringField would find no quoted value and insertStringField would add a *second* `monsters:` key — §AUDIT-03a's rot). **Duplicates are allowed, not rejected**, because `cat_quarter` ships one (`fluffy_cat` ×2) and a writer that cannot express what the corpus holds cannot round-trip it — it warns. **Then one `./api.sh put terrain sewers monsters=…` placed both void kin** (user-chosen: `sewers`' single node is **SFT "Visby Sewers"**, act 5, and Visby is where `VOID_TIDE_EVENTS[21]` sights the first Void Walker; levels 3 and 4 sit inside its `giant_rat`→`vampire` band). **Evidence:** the defect proved by round trip first (`ok:true` + `GET` agreeing + **0 occurrences in the file**); `git diff` on the game file for the placement = **exactly 1 line**; new `dx02h-terrain-roster-write.test.js` **6/6**, of which **5 fail on the stashed pre-fix tree** (restored, `shasum -c` OK — the one that passes is the inverse shape guard, already true); `check:walk` **15/15 exit 0**; 0 page errors. **The durable half is the inverse assertion** — *every* terrain roster entry must parse to a stat block, not a bare key string — because a roster written as JSON re-parses without error and is invisible to every other gate. **The lesson, now proved in three directions (create §DX-01c, delete §DX-01d/i, update §DX-02h): a write path's acceptance test is a ROUND TRIP, and "GET agrees with me" is not that test.** Docs: CONTRIBUTING Hazard **#6**;
