- [x] **§DX-02i — `XP_BY_TIER` deleted; it was not a stale copy of the shipped XP model, it was a different one ✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-03 `a047348`.** Premise held: one occurrence, its own declaration. **User chose delete over annotate**, and the divergence was measured before removing it rather than asserted: the shipped formula is `(S.enemy.ac || 10) * (S.opp.maxHp || 10)` (in `_grantMissEffortXp`, with the §XP-01 dials as percentages of that product), and against it the table paid **1.9×–6.5× less at every tier** — `deadly` a flat 1000 against a shipped mean of **4,095** across 33 monsters (min 1,649, max 12,012), `medium` 100 against a mean of 638. So adopting it would have silently cut the whole curve, not merely rescaled it. **Deleting it cost four dependents**, which is why the cheaper "comment it" option was on the table: `dx02g-monster-tier-contract.test.js`'s `TIER_KEYED_SITES` entry (would have failed *anchor not found*), `dx01e-anchor-convention.test.js`'s resolver fixture (**swapped to `const EFFORT_XP_PCT`**, a live §XP-01 dial with exactly one occurrence), `check-npcregs.js`'s `NOT_NPC_KEYED` classification, and `index.md`'s Design Constants row — **whose text was itself wrong**, claiming the table was *"kept for reference — L12+ uses `AC × maxHP`"* when nothing consulted it at **any** level. A ten-line retirement note stands where the declaration was, naming the shipped formula and the measured ratios, so the next session that greps it finds the reason instead of a tempting table. **Side correction on the path:** the comment two lines below read *"Level 20 = 680,000 XP"* while `XP_LEVELS` ends at **195,000** — no entry has ever been 680,000; corrected in place (`index.md` had the right number all along). **Evidence:** `check:walk` **15/15 exit 0** (`anchors:fix` refreshed the 23 hints my ±9-line shift drifted); the §DX-02i assertion in `dx02h-terrain-roster-write.test.js` **fails on the pre-fix tree**; full Playwright **750 passed / 4 failed**, the documented four. *(The illustrative `XP_BY_TIER → XP_BY_TIER_RETIRED` rename examples in `scripts/resolve-anchors.js`'s comments are left alone — they are hypotheticals, and §DX-01e's ship record depends on that name for continuity.)* *(Original row: )* `const XP_BY_TIER = { trivial:25, easy:50, medium:100, hard:250, deadly:1000 };` had **one** occurrence in the file — its own declaration. Battle XP is computed as ≈`AC·maxHP` (and the §XP-01 effort grants are percentages of that), so this table is not merely unused, it encodes a *different* XP model than the one that ships — the exact "authoritative-looking but fictional" shape §AUDIT-03l warns about, one grep away from being wired up by a future session that assumes it is live. Decide: delete it, or comment it as a retired model. Do **not** wire it in without a balance pass — 2,850 quests and the whole level curve are calibrated against the shipped formula. **Risk:** none (delete) / high if naively adopted.

- [x] **§DX-02l-FU ✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-04 `3684b67` — the assertion was narrowed to a runnable shape, and the standing Playwright baseline is FOUR reds again. The row's premise held exactly and its own preferred fix was the right one.** Confirmed the red at HEAD before touching anything (6 passed / 1 failed on a clean tree), and confirmed the row's diagnosis was **understated**: the loop aborts at its first failing file, so it reported `prompt.md:197` only — `CONTRIBUTING.md:145` (Hazard #7 itself) is a second hit the run never reached. **Shipped:** `curlInstructions(src, endpointRe)` replaces the line-level filter — it tracks fenced blocks (` ``` `/`~~~`), and outside a fence considers only **inline code spans** and a line that literally begins with `curl` (allowing a `$`/list-marker prefix). A *sentence* naming both tokens is prose and passes; a **copyable command** is an instruction and fails. Widened one notch in the row's own direction: the endpoint pattern is now `/api/(save|snapshots)` — `./api.sh snapshots` is a wrapper of the same vintage and was guarded by nothing. **The durable half is the positive control**, because narrowing an assertion is otherwise indistinguishable from deleting it: a second test runs the detector over four synthetic **instruction** shapes (fenced curl · inline-span curl · bare line · list-item `curl -XDELETE …/api/snapshots`) and asserts each is caught, then over four **prose** shapes (Hazard #7's own sentence · this test's own name in a doc bullet · *"use `./api.sh save`, never curl"* · a fenced curl to a *different* endpoint) and asserts none is. **Evidence:** file **8/8** (was 6/7); **negative control on a REAL doc** — appended a fenced `curl -s -XPOST …/api/save | jq` to `docs/api/api-user-guide.md`, the narrowed assertion went **red**, restored with `git checkout` + `shasum -c` **OK**; `check:walk` **15/15 exit 0**; full Playwright **771 passed / 4 failed / 0 flaky** = the `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` four alone, so the fifth red is retired and the 769/5 baseline recorded by §AUDIT-03k is superseded. **No doc prose was bent** — CONTRIBUTING Hazard #7 and prompt.md §7 still say what they said; `prompt.md` §7 records the retirement in the §DX-02e/§DX-02f house style. **The lesson, and the third instance of the class: the test was wrong, not the thing it tested** — and when a checker and a document disagree, the document that *records why the checker exists* is the last one you should rewrite. Original row follows. **§DX-02l-FU — the "no author-facing doc curls /api/save" assertion fails on PROSE about that very defect (found 2026-08-04 during §AUDIT-03k; a pre-existing red, not a regression).** `dx02l-save-snapshots-cli.test.js:190` flags any line in `api-user-guide.md` / `API-README.md` / `prompt.md` / `CONTRIBUTING.md` containing both `curl` and `/api/save`. **CONTRIBUTING Hazard #7 — written by §DX-02l itself in `91d3650` — now contains the sentence *"verifying §DX-02k needed raw `curl`"* on the same line as `POST /api/save`,** so the gate reads a historical account of the defect as an instruction to commit it. Proved pre-existing: the test fails at HEAD with a clean tree (stash-verified, `shasum -c` OK), so the §DX-02l ship record's *764 passed / 4 failed* predates its own last doc edit. **This makes the standing baseline FIVE reds, not four**, until it is fixed. **Fix shape — a design call, which is why it is a row and not a detour:** either narrow the assertion to an actual *instruction* shape (a fenced command line / a line starting with `curl`, not a sentence mentioning both tokens), or reword Hazard #7 so the two tokens never share a line. Prefer the first — the second makes the prose serve the regex, and this repo has already paid once for a doc bent to a checker. **Risk:** low (test or doc, no engine).

They are not a deliberate reserve: ~50 sit in the **Dark Fantasy Bestiary** block (`roll2hit-v3.html` `// ── Dark Fantasy Bestiary`), which is *partially* rostered — `fleder`, `cave_bear`, `koshchey`, `shaelmaar`, `ysbaddaden`, `garkain`, `slyzard` are orphaned while their immediate neighbours `protofleder`, `warg`, `alp`, `basilisk` are live — so this is an import that was wired up halfway, not a plan. The rest: `lemure`, `unicorn`, `black_bear`, `void_warlord`, `slyzard_matriarch`, `dragon_of_fyresdal`, `bruxa_corvo_bianco`. **Decide per group, not per monster** (rostering 53 one at a time is 53 design calls): the deadly named four read as boss/set-piece material wanting a `node.battle` or a quest, while the medium/hard bestiary bulk wants one or two terrain rosters. **The durable half is a gate**, and it needs the corpus clean first: a `check:monsterreach` in the §AUDIT-03j/n mould — **explicit** classification, an unlisted orphan FAILS — so a monster authored tomorrow cannot go unreachable silently. Note the trap that hid this class: `world.md` described a runtime injection (`_applyVoidPressureMonsters()`) that **does not exist**, so the doc made two orphans look rostered; re-measure, never read placement off a doc (§AUDIT-03l). **Risk:** low per roster write (`./api.sh put terrain … monsters=` now round-trips, §DX-02h), but the encounter-weight balance of a terrain changes with every addition — add a few, eyeball §7½, do not bulk-dump 50 into one roster.

- [x] **§DX-02k — the argless `save()` was not a stray test call: it was the server's own per-write path, and it stamped a 5.4 MB snapshot on every PUT ✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-03 `2ead185`.** The premise held — `save(outputPath)` fell back to `getStampedName()` and left dated files behind — but **both of the row's framings were wrong, and the measurement is what showed it.** **(1) The destination is the process CWD, not "next to the module."** `getStampedName()` returns a **bare filename**, and `fs.writeFileSync` resolves a bare name against `process.cwd()`. Proved by loading the repo's HTML from a scratch directory: the snapshot landed in the scratch directory. It read as "the repo root" only because that is where the server runs — a script run from anywhere else littered *there*, silently. **(2) The argless callers were not scripts. The SERVER was.** `saveAndRestart` and `saveAndVerify` — the persist step behind **every** successful PUT/POST/DELETE — ran `WBAPI.save()` argless, then `copyFileSync(snapshot, GAME_FILE)`, and **never removed the snapshot**. Two more argless sites sat in the batch endpoints. So the row's own proposed fix, *"make the argless form server-only,"* would have left the actual litter source untouched. And the snapshot was never a backup of the pre-write state — **it holds the NEW text**; its only job was to be the byte source of the copy, which makes it pure redundancy the moment the copy lands. **The check the row asked for came back the other way, too:** `archive-snapshots.sh` / `watch-snapshots.sh` / `monitor-snapshots.py` do depend on the stamped *name*, but `docs/notes/docs-dev-environment.md` documents their input as a **deliberate `cp`** — so the per-write stamping was not feeding a feature, it was drowning one: a curated session history turned into per-keystroke noise (patch chain last advanced **2026-07-09**, no watcher running, **6 orphans / ~32 MB** in the root). **Shipped:** `save(dest)` writes exactly where told and **refuses with no argument**, naming the alternative in the error · new **`WBAPI.saveStamped([dir])`** — the deliberate dated backup, landing **beside the source file** (`POST /api/save`, `wbapi save`, the audit-fix + junction-nuke endpoints; the last four also had the bare-name CWD bug) · new server-only **`saveGameFile()`** — temp beside the game file + **atomic rename**, so nothing is left to sweep *and* a reader can never catch a half-written game file; on failure the temp is kept and its path returned. **Evidence:** the CWD claim proved by a scratch-directory probe **before** any edit; **the pre-fix test run demonstrated the bug on itself** — stashing the fix and re-running dropped **two more 5.4 MB snapshots into the repo root**, which is the whole defect in one observation; new `dx02k-save-destination.test.js` **6/6**, of which **5 fail on the stashed pre-fix tree** (restored, `shasum -c` OK — the passer is the `save(dest)` round trip, already true); live server round trip = **3 successful writes** (`act=1` idempotent, `act=2`, `act=1` back), each verified on disk by grep and through `GET`, ending at the **original shasum** with **0 new snapshots and 0 temp files**; `POST /api/save` still returns a dated backup beside the game file, so the archive surface is intact; `check:walk` **15/15 exit 0**; full Playwright **756 passed / 4 failed** = the documented `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` four (+6 new tests over the 750 baseline), with `multiplayer-presence:171` **flaky, retried green** — a first run showed it red on both attempts and a second full run reproduced the documented baseline exactly, so it is the known flake, not a regression; root swept to **one** `roll2hit-v3*.html`, and two full suite runs added none back. **The durable half is the inverse assertion in the test: no shipped code may call `WBAPI.save()` with no destination.** **The lesson: "did it persist?" is only half of a write path's acceptance test — the other half is "and nowhere else."** A stray write throws nothing, changes no behaviour, and is found only by looking at the directory. Docs: **CONTRIBUTING Hazard #7**, `index.md` (Version Snapshots + `wbapi-core.js` persist surfaces), `docs/notes/docs-dev-environment.md`, `docs/api/api-user-guide.md` (which described `POST /api/save` as how writes reach disk — they already did), and annotations on the three historical notes that still traced the old stamped path (`lab-report-wbapi-architecture.md` §4.2, `data-code-migration-into-cells.md`, `cell-resume-prompts.md`). *(Original row: `save(outputPath)` falls back to `this.getStampedName()`, which writes a dated `roll2hit-v3-YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS.html` snapshot **next to the module — the repo root**. That is right for the server's autosave, but any test or one-shot script calling `save()` argless leaves a multi-MB file behind on every run; two such snapshots are sitting in the root right now from earlier sessions, invisible because the pattern is gitignored. `dx02h-terrain-roster-write.test.js` was fixed to pass `save(tmp)` explicitly. Decide: have `save()` refuse when the destination would be the repo root outside a server context, or add a `saveTo()` and make the argless form server-only; either way sweep the existing snapshots. **Risk:** low, but check `archive-snapshots.sh` / `watch-snapshots.sh` first — one of them may depend on the stamped-name behaviour.)*

- [x] **§DX-02l — the dated-backup surface had no `./api.sh` wrapper, and the sweep it wanted would have destroyed the history it exists to keep ✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-03 `91d3650`.** The premise held exactly as filed: `./api.sh save` answered `✗ Unknown command "save"`, `api/wb.js` held **0** occurrences of `save`/`snapshots`, and three lines of the *author-facing* guide (`api-user-guide.md` §17.13 and both worked-example workflows) told authors to reach the surface with `curl -s -XPOST …/api/save | jq` — the golden rule broken in the docs that teach it. **Shipped API-first, endpoint before wrapper (prompt.md §2.5):** `GET /api/snapshots` (the dir, per-file size/mtime, total, and whether the patch chain already holds it) and `DELETE /api/snapshots` (nonce-guarded like every other destructive route), then `./api.sh save` and `./api.sh snapshots [--sweep] [--force]`. `snapshot` had to be added to the nonce endpoint's `validTypes` — it is the one type with **no collection behind it**, since the sweep destroys files, not entries, so the key resolution is skipped rather than run against `undefined`. **The row's framing of the sweep was the thing that changed on contact.** It called the disposal *"a `rm` a human has to remember"* — but `archive-snapshots.sh` **already disposes of them**, and it does so by diffing each snapshot into a `milepoints/patches` delta and *then* `rm`-ing the file. So a blunt sweep is not a chore automated, it is **the patch chain's input deleted before it is read**: an unarchived snapshot is the only copy of that state. `--sweep` therefore deletes only what the chain already holds (`<stem>.patch` present, or named by `_last.name`) and **names `./archive-snapshots.sh` in the skip reason**; `--force` is the deliberate discard. **Evidence:** live round trip on the real server before any test — `./api.sh save` → 5.2 MB backup beside the game file → `snapshots` listed it `unarchived` → `--sweep` **kept it** and said why → `--sweep --force` freed it, with `roll2hit-v3.html` at its **original shasum** and the root back to one file; new `dx02l-save-snapshots-cli.test.js` **7/7**, and **all 7 fail on the stashed pre-fix tree** (restored, `shasum -c` OK — nothing here is vacuous); `check:walk` **15/15 exit 0**; full Playwright **764 passed / 4 failed** = the documented `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` four, **0 flaky**. **No HTML shipped and no §7½ — authoring surface only; nothing a player sees changed.** **The durable half is what the sweep is allowed to name:** a decoy suite (`roll2hit-v3-notes.html`, `-2026.html`, `-20260709.html`, `-20260709-0341.html`, `.htm`, `.html.bak`) survives a **forced** sweep beside the game file, because only an exact `-YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS.html` stamp is reachable — the stamp is the entire guard between *delete the backups* and *delete the game*. The test also pins the row's own lesson: **no author-facing doc may reach `/api/save` with `curl`.** **The lesson: an endpoint whose only documented client is `curl` is not a finished write path — the wrapper is what makes it real, and "it has no wrapper" is usually visible in the docs before it is visible in the CLI.** Docs: `docs/api/api-user-guide.md` (§17.13 rewritten + both workflows + the endpoint card), `API-README.md` (new *Dated backups* section + 3 mapping rows), `wbapi-help.md`, `docs/notes/docs-dev-environment.md`, `index.md`, `prompt.md` §3 + §9, **CONTRIBUTING Hazard #7** (which now carries the reporting half: the directory is gitignored, so `./api.sh snapshots` is the only thing that will ever tell you what is in it), and `./api.sh help` gained a *Persistence* block under SERVER LIFECYCLE saying plainly that you do **not** need `save` after a write. *(Original row: )* Verifying that the dated-backup surface still worked needed a raw `curl -X POST http://localhost:1367/api/save`, because `./api.sh save` answers `✗ Unknown command "save"`. prompt.md §3's golden rule is that authoring never falls back to raw `curl` — *"if `./api.sh` lacks a command you need, add the endpoint + a named `./api.sh` wrapper"* — and here the endpoint exists while the wrapper does not, which is the same gap read from the other end. Small: an `api/wb.js` command that POSTs `/api/save` and prints the `backup` / `primary` paths. Worth doing together with a `./api.sh snapshots` (list/sweep the root's dated files) now that they are produced only on request — the sweep is currently a `rm` a human has to remember. **Risk:** trivial; read-modify of the CLI dispatch table only, no game-file writes.


- [x] **§DX-01d — the junction-node CLI hazards ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-30 `2f467f3` (closes §DX-01i in the same increment). Hazards #3/#4 are retired and a new Hazard #5 records the defect class.** Taken as the board's named "unambiguous pairing" while §AUDIT-03d's design call stays open. **The row's `del node J##` half was right about the symptom and wrong about the scope, and that was the finding:** measured in-process against a *copy* of the shipped file (real game file never written), `monster:fish_01`, `quest:mq_1` **and** `node:RUH` each returned `ok:true`, each left `_rawSrc` **byte-identical**, and each **came back after save+reload**. It was never junction-specific and never monster-specific — **every `delete()` in `wbapi-core.js` was the single line `delete WBAPI.<collection>[key]`**, and the server's generic `DELETE` handler ended at `json(...)` with **no `saveAndVerify` at all**, so nothing reached disk even in principle. `WBAPI.deleteNodeSource` — the one function that *did* patch the source — was **dead code with zero callers** (and its private brace-walker missed `/* */` comments and left the `NODE_COORDS` row orphaned anyway). **This is the exact mirror of §DX-01c: a write path that reports success without persisting. Same standing lesson both directions — the failure is silent because nothing ever throws.**
  **Shipped (1) the deleter:** `WBAPI.deleteEntrySource(section, key)` — one source-level excision for all four types, **verify-or-revert** (after the splice the section's depth-1 key *multiset* must differ by exactly one instance of `key`, else the splice is rolled back and the delete fails loudly instead of corrupting the section), clamped past nested START anchors so a `MONSTER_POOL` delete can never reach into the trophy-drops map (§DX-01c's real-but-wrong-object trap, mirrored). **(2) one scanner, not two:** `check-dupkeys.js`'s proven comment/string/regex-safe `scanTokens` **moved into `wbapi-core.js`** and is imported back by the gate (`WBAPI._scanTokens`), so the audit gate and the writer can never disagree about what an entry is — a private copy would drift, which is the §AUDIT-03f silent-drop class again. **(3) cascades:** a node takes its `NODE_COORDS` row, a monster takes its `MONSTER_DROPS` trophy — no orphan left for `./api.sh audit`. **(4) the server persists and proves it:** the generic `DELETE` now routes through `saveAndVerify` with a new **`postVerify`** hook (a delete's proof of success is an *absence* in the re-parsed collections, which the `expectedFields` path cannot express) and returns `deleteVerified:true`, or a 500 if the entry survived. **(5) `highway --execute` deprecated and refused**, up front before the coordinate fetch (so it costs nothing and needs no server), with the real recipe in the error: `ROAD_RUNS` + `build-roads.js --apply`, and `./api.sh reachability` as the authority for why waypoints were never necessary. `deleteNodeSource` kept as a thin delegating wrapper. **Verified:** new `tests/integration/dx01di-delete-persists.test.js` **8/8** — round trip per type, both cascades, **the 20 largest nested UQF bodies each excised individually** (largest 3,350 bytes of braces/strings/arrow-fns; each must cost exactly one entry), verify-or-revert, the `blockedBy` guards still blocking with the source byte-identical, and the highway refusal. **End-to-end through the live server, twice, each a byte-identical no-op:** `post monster dx01i_probe` → `del monster` → `grep -c` **0** (the row's exact reproduction, which used to leave **1**); `post node ZZP` → `del node` → **0**; game file `shasum 018ee943…` **unchanged** both times. `check:walk` **12/12 green (exit 0)**, `check:dupkeys --selftest` 4/4 on the relocated scanner. Full Playwright **717 passed / 4 failed** — the baseline is **exactly** the documented four in `worldbuilder-crud-arrays.test.js`, plus the known `multiplayer-presence` flake (passed on retry); 717 = the 710 baseline + these 8, minus the flake counted separately. **Docs synced:** CONTRIBUTING.md Hazards #2/#3/#4 updated and a new **Hazard #5** written to keep the class (*whenever you add a write path, the acceptance test is a round trip — save, re-parse, assert the change survived*), prompt.md §3 hazards #4/#5, `api-user-guide.md` §10 (cascade/verify/guard table), `API-README.md`, and `./api.sh help` — whose §12 `del` section now states the guarantee and whose §21 no longer advertises **eleven** `highway --execute` invocations that would die. *(Original row: "Fix or deprecate the junction-node CLI hazards — both documented as Hazards #3/#4 and the upstream cause of §DX-01a's J14/J15 reds; `highway --execute` drops sparse `junction:true` nodes and zero road cells, `del node J##` desyncs model from file.")*

- [x] **§DX-01e — Retire line-number anchor drift ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-31 `57d79d7`. The row asked for a convention; the measurement is what made the case: 43 of the 50 anchors in the live docs were stale, and every single one of CONTRIBUTING.md's ten.** **The convention:** an anchor is one token, `` `symbol@1234` `` — the **symbol** is the pointer (any literal substring of the line meant, optionally file-qualified: `` `js/wbapi-server.js:function seededNext@1147` ``), the **number is a cached hint**. That is §DX-02e/§DX-02f's lesson in a third place: *pin the property, never the generated coordinate* — a line number is generated by every edit above it. **Measured before building:** of 14 bare anchors sampled at HEAD, **9 pointed at unrelated code** (`22104`, cited for `_rollSkill`, had become a prof-bonus line; `31618`, cited for the §CELL-03 position sync, an `aurosBlueprintKnown` branch); repo-wide the bare form appears **720** times across 27 files. **Shipped:** `scripts/resolve-anchors.js` — audit (exit 1 on findings) · `--fix` · `--legacy` (migration tracker) · a lookup mode that replaces `grep -n` (`node scripts/resolve-anchors.js execBits`) · `--selftest`; **`check:walk` gate #15 `check:anchors`**; `npm run anchors` / `anchors:fix`. **The gate's failure condition is deliberately narrow: a DEAD symbol fails, a drifted number only warns.** Failing on drift would re-create the per-increment tax the row exists to remove, and `anchors:fix` refreshes all of them in one command. Resolution is **identifier-boundary aware** — found while building the negative control: plain substring matching let `XP_BY_TIER` → `XP_BY_TIER_RETIRED` still 'resolve', so a *rename*, the exact thing the gate is for, would have passed. **Migrated (the live docs only):** CONTRIBUTING.md 10 · mechanics.md 3 · docs/mechanics/mechanics-combat.md 2 · **every bare anchor in BACKLOG.md's OPEN rows, 0 left**. Closed ship records, `plan-archive.md` (211) and the lab reports (~300) keep theirs — **history is annotated, not rewritten** (§DX-02c/§AUDIT-03m precedent). `potential.md`'s 36 are the only live-doc remainder → filed as **§DX-01e-FU**. **Two stale claims surfaced by re-anchoring and corrected in place** (both in CONTRIBUTING's Host/Script policy, both about work that has since shipped): the seeded-RNG bullet still said *"the client rolls the same encounter from `Math.random()` … until §VM-01-B lands"* — §VM-01-B landed, the client draws `_seededNext()`, and what still diverges is the *weighting*, not the stream; and the `_legacy_fn` bullet cited `quest_1367_f_plague` as *"rolls `Math.random() > 0.5` inside quest data"* when **no `_legacy_fn` line in the file contains `Math.random` any more**. **Evidence:** 6-phase `--selftest` (dead · stale · fresh · fix · boundary · qualified); **negative control on the real game file** — renamed `const XP_BY_TIER` in place, gate exits 1 naming `BACKLOG.md:312`, file restored and `shasum -c` **OK**; `dx01e-anchor-convention.test.js` **4/4**, and the migration test **fails on the pre-migration docs** (`git stash` of the 4 doc files → 1 failed / 3 passed); `check:walk` **15/15 exit 0**; the audit reports **50 anchors across 4 docs, 0 dead**. **No HTML shipped and no §7½** — docs + tooling only; nothing a player sees changed. *(Original row: BACKLOG.md carries 182 bare 5-digit line anchors; they rot on every HTML edit, and nearly every §VM-01 row ends with "re-grep the anchors before the next edit — they will drift." Propose a lightweight, durable convention + a tiny `scripts/resolve-anchors.js`. **Payoff:** removes a per-increment tax and a whole class of stale-pointer bugs. **Risk:** low; adopt incrementally, no big-bang rewrite.)*

`potential.md` rule 3 carries a dated pass note stating plainly that **only the anchored claims were re-measured** — the rest is still `43bd09c`-era. **Side finding filed, not swept: §DX-01e-FU2.** *(Original row: `potential.md` is the live seed inbox* — its anchors are *read to build from*, so they carry the same stale-pointer risk the parent row just retired, and they were measured at `43bd09c` (the §POT-PROMOTE preamble already warns several had drifted by 2026-07-23). Everything else still on the bare form is **history** (`plan-archive.md` 211 · the lab reports ~300 · closed ship records) and is deliberately left alone — annotate, don't rewrite. Method: name what each anchor points at, keep the old number, then `npm run anchors:fix` (the tool refuses to guess a number for a symbol it cannot find, so a dead pointer surfaces as a gate failure rather than a silent re-point). Re-run `npm run anchors --legacy` after to see the remaining count. **Risk:** none (doc-only, gate-fenced).)*

### §AUDIT-03 — Quest-data appended-field rot (NEW 2026-07-28, surfaced by §VM-01-G3)

> §VM-01-G3 found that the bulk audit waves `a721254` ("80+ passText/failText/activateNode/desc/hint fields patched") and `ea02faf` ("wire npc field on all 985 quests") APPENDED fields to entries that already declared them — JS last-key-wins silently flipped authored values — and `710bb75` ("Fix 8 activateNode errors: SF→LCY, CQ→CDG, FR→AMS") remapped at least two dead codes to the WRONG target. G3 fixed the ~20 quests in its five arcs; these rows are the corpus-wide sweeps. All are data-only audits — grep/parse first, fix through `./api.sh` where expressible (⚠️ Hazard #1 for hand-edits).

- [x] **§AUDIT-03a ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-28 — `check:dupkeys` is `check:walk` gate #11; the 2 corpus survivors fixed.** New `scripts/check-dupkeys.js`: string/template/comment-safe token scanner over all 11 WORLDBUILDER sections (~4,800 entries), stack-of-maps per object literal so a duplicate key at ANY nesting is caught while sibling array elements/function-body braces never false-positive; `--selftest` proves clean-pass + planted-dup-caught. **Corpus findings: exactly 2 survivors** (beyond G3's arcs): `quest_la_riva_01` + `quest_no_fishing_sign`, both authored `activateNode:null` + appended `activateNode:"CDG"` — both quests are CODE-activated (la_riva_01 by the Cat-King-defeat handler with Kenickie's staged monologue; no_fishing_sign by the coupon pull-tab), so the shadowing append mass-activated them at CDG, bypassing their staging. Fixed per the G3 pattern: appended line removed, authored `null` kept (hand-edit — dup-removal isn't expressible via PUT; server confirmed stopped, Hazard #1). Their `npc:"long_john_silver_sen"` stamps left for §AUDIT-03b (scope fence). **Wired into `package.json` `check:walk` (selftest + audit), which is the single CI step in walk-invariants.yml — an append can never silently flip an authored value again.** Evidence: `check:walk` all 11 gates green; `quest-runtime-uqf` 303/303; prompt.md §7 gate count synced 10→11. Note: MONSTER_POOL count reads 796 because that section legitimately holds both `MONSTER_POOL` + `MONSTER_DROPS` objects (2×398 — both audited); ITEM_DB is genuinely empty; FISH_POOL is array-rooted (fields audited at element level, entry count reads 0 — cosmetic).
- [x] **§AUDIT-03b ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-29 — all 203 mis-stamped quests re-anchored to their real NPC; the vocabulary and the field patcher fixed underneath.** `ea02faf` had bulk-defaulted `npc:"long_john_silver_sen"` onto **203** quests. **Established first (step 1, prior session): `q.npc` is authoring metadata only** — the game client has ZERO quest-level `.npc` reads, so no gameplay risk and no golden-state diff was needed; consumers are worldbuilder display, `_questsByNpc`, `advise` (unresolvable key ⇒ *warning*), `audit` (**missing** field ⇒ *error* — the pressure that caused the bulk default), and the NPC delete-guards.
  **Result: 203/203 re-anchored correctly · exactly the 7 genuine Silver quests kept (`sen_c1a2/a3`, `sen_c4a1/a2/a3`, `sen_c7a1/a2` — every scene is built on him) · 196 changed · 0 unresolved · 0 collateral.**
  **Three defects found and fixed on the way (the row's own premises included):**
  1. **The accepted vocabulary was too narrow.** `_npcOk` (duplicated in two `wbapi-server.js` closures) and `advise` accepted only BIRKA_NPC + normalized `NODE_MAP` inline `npc` — so real, rendered speakers living in `NPC_DIALOGUES` (`jimmy`/`solvak`/`yva`/`rennau`/`kenickie`/`isolde_voss`/`benedikt_rasp`/`sandy_cat`) and the 20 `EB_NPC_DIALOGUE` quest-givers would all have advise-warned. Replaced with **one shared `WBAPI.npcKeyOk` / `npcKeyVocab()` in `js/wbapi-core.js`** over four registries (BIRKA_NPC · NODE_MAP inline · NPC_DIALOGUES · EB_NPC_DIALOGUE), both server closures now delegate to it, and `EB_NPC_DIALOGUE` is parsed for the first time (it lives OUTSIDE the WORLDBUILDER markers, so it is read off the raw source). **Vocabulary 270 → 297 keys**; a bogus key is still rejected.
  2. **`patchStringField` was not depth-aware — a silent no-op class.** The first batch reported `patched:196, errors:[]` but **6 writes never landed**: `editField('quest', id, 'npc', …)` matched the FIRST `npc:` token anywhere in the entry, which for `quest_wm_02/wm_03/tl_01/tl_03/vs_01/vs_02` is the **nested** `{ kind:'favor', npc:'…' }` inside `onComplete`. Those six happened to have a target key equal to the nested value, so the write was value-preserving (quote style only) — but any quest where they differed would have had its **favor bit rewritten instead**. Fixed with a new `firstTopLevelMatch(body, re)` helper: a single string- and comment-aware pass builds a depth map, and only a match at brace depth 0 of the entry body can be chosen (comment-awareness per the §AUDIT-03f lesson). Re-ran the 6 → all correct, favor bits untouched. **Proof of zero collateral across the whole corpus: all 16 `kind:'favor', npc:` values byte-identical before vs after (git-diff extraction), and the game-file diff is exactly 196 insertions / 196 deletions.**
  3. **The row's `hunt2 → tessie` derivation was wrong.** Tessie is not an `EB_NPC_DIALOGUE` giver — enumerating the object gives 20 givers and she is absent; she exists only in `quest_hunt2_*` prose. Re-anchored `hunt2_01–04` to **`the_elder_fisherwoman`**, the §BOARD-01-FU6 referral source that hands the WRO thread off (the `hunt_01` narrative that names Tessie's road).
  **The ~12 OPEN per-quest calls were resolved by reading each quest's text** (recorded in the mapping script): `spark_01/02/05`→`magistra_elara_muffat` (LCY anchor; Inspector Wren-Pembury/"Aldous" has no key) · `spark2_01–05`→`mairén_fionn` (DNF anchor; Commissioner Fehn has no key) · `wis_00/06/07`→`solvak` and `wis_04`→`warlord_kael_mordus` and `alch_02`→`elder_fionn` (Roen has no key anywhere — §AUDIT-03i) · `wis_03`→`captain_vera_keel` (GCI's own NPC; the text says "Keel talked about the commission") · `vs_warden`→`solvak` · `va_01–04`→`benedikt_rasp` (va_04's text names him outright) · `ng_01–03`→`city_guard_captain` (LHR anchor) · `ca_01`→`magistra_elara_muffat` · BOO trio + `no_fishing_sign`→`the_fisherman` · `shore_01`→`the_tide_reader` and `sunken_02`/`depth_01`→`the_stone_inscription` (chain-sibling consistency) · `cat_06`→`jimmy_two-tails` ("Tommy" has no key) · `solm_01`→`proprietor_dusk` (the Map Shop scene) · `mol001_act2`→`bolli_bollason` (his household's men hold the ford) and `mol001_act3`→`gudrun_osvifr` (Helgi Osvifsson unkeyed → the chain namesake, = act1's own stamp). The anchoring rule is now written into `quest.md`.
  **API-first honored:** the purpose-built `POST /api/batch/npc` endpoint existed but had no wrapper, so **`./api.sh batch-npc <updates.json>`** was added to `api/wb.js` (+ both help blocks) — one parse and one save for the whole batch instead of 196 full-file rewrites. Hazard #1 respected throughout: the `js/*.js` edits were made with the server stopped, then the server was restarted on a fresh PID before each write session, and the `_monsterLevel` signature survived both saves.
  **Evidence:** `check:walk` **11/11 green** (exit 0, incl. `check:dupkeys` + the §AUDIT-03f parse-parity phase) · `quest-runtime-uqf` + `npc-card-map` **325 passed** · new permanent `tests/integration/audit03b-npc-anchor.test.js` **4/4** (the 7-keep corpus assertion · the only-10-tolerated-unresolvable-keys pin · the 4-registry `npcKeyOk` · the depth-aware-patcher regression) · `node --check` clean on `wbapi-core.js`/`wbapi-server.js`/`wb.js` · quest count **2,853** unchanged. Docs synced: `quest.md` gained an "`npc` field — authoring metadata, not gameplay" section (the four registries + the anchoring rule + the `batch-npc` recipe). Side findings logged as **§AUDIT-03g/h/i** below rather than fixed inline.

Only **`SF → LCY` was wrong** — already fixed by §VM-01-G3 (`SF` = the Storefront = `STN`). **The prior session's open question — "should one arc sit at two nodes?" — is answered and moot:** the La Riva arc is legitimately **bi-nodal** (Kenickie sends you east from `CDG` · Connie + Aldo at `AMS` · the account book goes back to Kenickie at `CDG`, hence `la_riva_03`'s `waypointNode:'CDG'`), and **5 of the 8 remapped lines no longer exist** — they were the appended duplicates §AUDIT-03a / §VM-01-G3 removed, so all three `la_riva` quests are `activateNode:null`, staged by code (`01` by the `CQ_KING` defeat handler, `02`/`03` by the AMS hooks). **The audit's real product — a corpus scan: 0 of 2,853 quests name a node that does not exist**, and it covered **`waypointNode`, which the original audit never checked at all.** **One data fix:** `quest_cat_06` was anchored to `jimmy_two-tails` (CDG's inline-name slug) while its three siblings use the `NPC_DIALOGUES` profile key `jimmy` — one character filed under two `_questsByNpc` headings (§AUDIT-03k); collapsed to `jimmy` via an in-process `WBAPI.editField` write (the §TIDE-01-A path, byte-identical to the server's PUT; no daemon, per the no-background-processes rule). **Most likely source of the whole class, measured and filed as §AUDIT-03l: `maps.md`'s LEGEND table — the doc an author reads to look up a node code — has 73 of 76 rows naming codes that do not exist** (it predates the airport-code naming; its `Node #` column is still correct and is the recovery key). The circumstantial case is strong: those three codes appear nowhere else, and `quest_tl_01` carries `activateNode:"SF"` beside a live `waypointNode:'STN'` **for the same node** — one field read off the table, the other off `NODE_MAP`. Neutralized today with a hazard banner above the table; the remap is its own increment. **Evidence:** golden parse diff across all 7 collections = **exactly 1 entry diff, `quest_cat_06`, `fields=npc` alone**, 0 lost / 0 gained, still 2,853 quests; new permanent `tests/integration/audit03c-node-refs.test.js` **4/4** (both fields resolve · the 3 dead codes stay dead + the labels that identify them · the 8 audited quests hold their re-derived targets · cat_06 on the profile key), with a **negative control** (re-planting `activateNode:"SF"` + `waypointNode:"CQ"` in memory — both caught, so the guard is not vacuous); `check:walk` **11/11 exit 0**; **367 Playwright green, exit 0 both runs** (`quest-runtime-uqf` + panels/hooks/activation 322/322 · the audit/NPC set 45/45). No §7½ (nothing player-visible — `quest.npc` has zero quest-level client reads per §AUDIT-03b, and no node reference moved). Docs: `quest.md` (both arc sections + the `npc` section's six measured alias pairs), `world.md` §XIX, `maps.md` banner.

Fix: comments copy through verbatim in `removeFns`' main loop + both fn-body scanners (`js/wbapi-core.js`). Sweep: cross-reference every `activateNode` value against entries lacking `code`, add the missing `code` fields (or backfill `code` = key at parse time, one line in the loader — likely the better fix; check `_getNodeMapColor`-class consumers first).

The mechanism is confirmed end-to-end: `storyRender` passes `NODE_MAP[S_story.currentCode]`, `storyCheckQuests` → `_uqfActivateAtNode(node)` → `_questsByNode(node.code)` — so at a code-less node `node.code` is `undefined` and the arrival-activation lookup returns nothing. Spot-checked code-less nodes include **BK, SPB, KMS, ZVD, FBR** (the whole §KG corridor) while LHR/VS/TL/CDG do carry `code`. ⚠️ **2,285 is an upper bound on the damage, not a count of dead quests** — many of those quests reach `active` by another path (the `unlock` opcode, a code handler, a chain sibling), and that is exactly what the sweep must separate. Four of the seven pairs are corroborated independently by `NODE_NPC_KEYS`' own node→key column (LHR→`yael`, TLL→`brynn`, MHQ→`quill`, LLA→`pachelbel`), the other three by `profile.node` or an exact name match. **Shipped:** `WBAPI.NPC_ALIASES` + `npcCanonicalKey()` in `js/wbapi-core.js`; the seven slugs **removed from `npcKeyVocab()`** (297→290) so a quest still carrying one advise-warns instead of validating alongside the real key; `editField` collapses **(quest, npc) only** on write — scoped deliberately, since normalizing `NODE_MAP`'s own inline npc would be the §AUDIT-03h bug; `_buildIndexes` indexes `_questsByNpc` under the canonical key, which is what covers a **hand-authored nested UQF block** (Hazard #3 never touches the API); the create path normalizes before the world-logic check (a create serializes a fresh literal and never reaches `editField`); and every write path now **reports** the collapse (`aliased` on PUT/batch, `aliasedNpc` on create) rather than silently rewriting what the author sent. **Corpus:** the 5 `city_guard_captain` quests — the `quest_ng_01/02/03` Froberger set, `quest_sir_jullean`, `quest_courier_release` — collapsed onto `yael` via `./api.sh batch-npc`, so she is one person again at **22 quests** (was 17 + 5). **A defect found on the way, in the verifier rather than the writer:** `saveAndVerify` compared the reloaded field against **what the caller sent**, so the first live PUT returned `ok:false — field mismatch after reload: npc` on a write that was entirely correct — the inverse of this repo's usual failure. It now verifies against what `editField` actually wrote. **Evidence:** the collapse was sent **as the alias** (`npc=city_guard_captain`) and `yael` landed on disk — the round trip IS the acceptance test; `git diff` on the HTML = **5 lines, nothing else**; live round trips on all three write paths (batch → 5 patched · PUT → `aliased{city_guard_captain→yael}` + `verified ok` · POST create → `aliasedNpc{commander_bruhns→auros}`), probe quest deleted with 0 residue; `./api.sh audit` **errors 0**; `check:walk` **15/15 exit 0** with the gate's own `--selftest` catching **6/6** planted defects including both new alias shapes; new `audit03k-npc-aliases.test.js` **6/6**, and **all 6 fail on the stashed pre-fix tree** (restored, `shasum -c` OK); the npc/audit family **29/29**. **Two sibling tests were stale after the collapse and moved with it, annotated not rewritten:** `audit03g`'s ANCHORED map (the derivation had been right about the character and wrong about the key — the 68 total is unchanged) and `audit03c`'s `jimmy_two-tails` assertion, which said in so many words *"the slug still resolves — this is §AUDIT-03k, not a broken key"* and now asserts the opposite on purpose. **The durable half is gate #14 phase 5**, because nothing else can see this class: the key resolves, the write succeeds, the game is correct, and only an authoring surface nobody looks at is wrong. It fails on any authored `npc:` alias **and** on a NEW inline display name that collides with a live profile and is classified in neither `NPC_ALIASES` nor `NOT_AN_ALIAS`. **The lesson: a key that resolves can still be the wrong key — "does it exist?" and "is it the one this character is filed under?" are different questions, and only the second one notices a person split in two.** Docs: `quest.md` §"The `npc` field" (the 7-row alias table + the role-collision rule + the corrected anchoring row), `prompt.md` §7 (gate #14 gains phase 5), `index.md` (gate row + `wbapi-core` NPC-identity surface). **Side finding filed, not swept: §DX-02l-FU** (a pre-existing red, proved at HEAD without this row's changes). Original row follows. **§AUDIT-03k — one character, two accepted `npc` keys (found 2026-07-29 during §AUDIT-03g).** Guard Captain **Yael Scheidemann** is reachable as *both* `yael` (registry 1 — the `BIRKA_NPC` profile at LHR, occupation "city guard captain") *and* `city_guard_captain` (registry 2 — LHR's `NODE_MAP` inline npc string). Both pass `npcKeyOk`, and the corpus already splits: `yael` on `mq_1`/`sq_battling`/`sq_leveling`/`sq_2` + the 8 §AUDIT-03g anchors, `city_guard_captain` on the `quest_ng_01/02/03` Froberger set + the 2 §AUDIT-03g desk-guard scenes. So `_questsByNpc` indexes one person under two headings. The same shape holds wherever a profile and its node's inline string name the same NPC (`brynn`/`innkeeper_brynn`, `archivus_sweelinck`/`archivus_ptolemy_sweelinck`, `auros`/`commander_bruhns` — all four keys live in the 297-key vocabulary today). **Fix shape:** teach `npcKeyVocab()` an alias map (inline-string key → profile key) and normalize on write, so registry 2 resolves *to* registry 1 instead of alongside it; then one `batch-npc` pass to collapse the existing splits. **Risk:** low-medium (touches the shared vocabulary that `advise`/`audit`/the delete-guards all read; needs the §AUDIT-03b tests green after).

More importantly the row's recipe — *"look each `Node #` up and rewrite the code column"* — would have produced a **half-true** table: the `Grid Cell` column is the retired **26×16** projection while live `NODE_COORDS` runs **r 2–73 / c 154–249**, so remapping only the codes would have left an authoritative-looking doc whose coordinates are fiction. And `num` alone is **not** a sufficient key: **num 77 is held by two nodes** (`MJF` and `CDG`) and several terrain keys were renamed (`epic_goblin`→`epic_goblin_cave`, `desert_caravan`→`arabia`), so every match needs terrain/label corroboration. **Shipped instead — generate + quarantine:** new **`scripts/node-index.js`** / **`npm run nodes`** emits **`docs/maps/node-index.md`** from the same `wbapi-core` parse the `:1367` server reads (the `npm run stats` §DX-01g pattern) — all **416** nodes with code · `Node #` · terrain · act · **live cell** · 🛏 · label · inline NPC, plus a **LEGACY CODE MAP** (**83 recovered · 11 unresolved**) that is the Rosetta stone for every older doc, lab report and commit message. It auto-flags the nastiest class: **a legacy code that IS a live key but for a different node** — **`CI`** (Birka's streets historically; the *Chancery Court* live) and **`BK`** (the *Broken Tooth Tavern* = `VBY` historically; *Birka Shore* live). A naive "does this code resolve?" check passes on both. **New `check:walk` gate #12, `check:nodeindex`** — the committed index must be byte-identical to a fresh generation, so it cannot rot the way the legend did. **`maps.md` restructured:** a READ-THIS-FIRST table at the top routing every live question to its live source, the node-code section replaced by a pointer to the generated index, and **all six 26×16-era sections kept verbatim but re-headed ⚠️ HISTORICAL** (TINY MAP · FULL MAP · LEGEND · NODE NETWORK · COORDINATE INDEX · SLEEP NODES — the last of which listed 8 of the live **38** `sleep:true` checkpoints). **`index.md`:** the Town Cross-Reference remapped to live keys, with two corrections beyond the rename — its **Inn** column named `SF`/`IS`, *neither of which is a `sleep:true` node* (the real checkpoints are `SEN` and `NAS`), and Visby's `BK` was the Broken Tooth Tavern = `VBY`, not the live `BK`; `VENDOR_NODES` corrected `BA/MQ/SF/IS/BK` → `LLA/LGW/STN/PDL/BK`; the doc map gained the generated file. **Evidence:** `check:walk` **12/12 exit 0**; new `tests/integration/audit03l-node-index.test.js` **5/5** (index ≡ fresh generation · every live node present with live num+terrain · every LEGACY CODE MAP target resolves *and is justified by the legacy `Node #`* · both collisions stay flagged · `maps.md` routes to the index and marks its own tables historical); **two negative controls** — appending a bogus row to the committed index, and inserting a real node into `NODE_MAP` — each made gate #12 exit 1, and the game file was restored byte-identical (`git diff` empty). Docs synced: `prompt.md` §4 (the *never read a code off a doc table* authoring rule), §7 (11→12 gates), §9 cheat sheet. **Follow-up left open: §AUDIT-03m** below. Original row follows. **§AUDIT-03l — the docs answer "what is this node's code?" with codes that do not exist (found 2026-07-29 during §AUDIT-03c; this is the most likely *cause* of the 03c bug, not a sibling of it).** `maps.md`'s **LEGEND — Two-Letter Code Reference** is the table an author consults to write an `activateNode`, and **73 of its 76 rows name a code with no `NODE_MAP` entry.** It was written before the world adopted airport codes and was never migrated: `SL`→`BMA` · `CI`→`LHR` · `IN`→`TLL` · `TV`→`MHQ` · `BA`→`LLA` · `CR`→`KRN` · `CY`→`HKG` · `DK`→`LCY` · `MQ`→`LGW` · **`SF`→`STN`** · `MS`→`SEN` · `AL`→`GOT` · **`CQ`→`CDG`** · `AT`→`RAI` · … (full list reproducible in seconds — see the recipe below). **The `Node #` column is still correct and is the recovery key:** every legacy row maps to exactly one live key by `NODE_MAP[k].num`, so the remap is mechanical, not a judgment call. Three traps: (a) **`CI` resolves** — but to the Chancery Court (num 429), not Birka's streets, so a naive "does the code exist?" check passes while the row is still wrong; (b) `RD` (num 78) has **no live node at all**; (c) the table's **⚠️ PLANNED markers are stale** — the `CQ` row still says the Ally Cat arc is *"not yet in HTML"* when 7 quests have shipped at `CDG` for months. **Blast radius beyond that table:** the same legacy codes appear in `maps.md`'s ASCII region grid + adjacency tables, `index.md`'s region table (`CQ`, `SF`) and `VENDOR_NODES` line (says `BA/MQ/SF/IS/BK`; live is `LLA/LGW/STN/PDL/BK`), `world.md`, and `story.md` — plus the `Q-FR-01`-style **design IDs**, which are quest identifiers and should be **left alone**. **Recipe:** parse the table rows, look each `Node #` up in `WBAPI.nodeMap` by `num`, and rewrite the code column (script kept in the §AUDIT-03c session notes; it printed all 73 in one pass). **Consider a `check:walk` gate** asserting every node code cited in a docs table resolves — this pairs naturally with §AUDIT-03j's proposed registry-key gate. **Interim mitigation shipped 2026-07-29:** a hazard banner above the table naming the drift, the recovery key, and the `710bb75` incident it caused. **Risk:** low (docs only) but **high value** — this is a live authoring trap that has already produced one wrong-city bug that survived two months.

an explanatory comment above the seven tables in the §AUDIT-03j house style; **new `scripts/check-npcregs.js` = `check:walk` gate #14**, the npc mirror of #13 — 4 phases (registry keys incl. one nested path `TOWN_CRIER_LINES.npcs`; **explicit** classification of all 36 all-lowercase top-level tables across `NPC_KEYED`/`VOCAB_SOURCE`/`NOT_NPC_KEYED`, an unlisted one FAILS; `_npcFavor('…')`/`npcFavorability['…']` literals; `npcOrder` arrays). **It deliberately does NOT check npc-*valued* fields** — `NODE_MAP`'s inline `npc` is *supposed* to be a display name (§AUDIT-03h's inverse lesson), and quest anchors are already pinned by `audit03h-npc-normalize`. **Evidence:** `check:walk` **14/14 exit 0**; 4-phase `--selftest`; **negative control with the real historical defects** — the gate run against the pre-fix file named **all 29**, exactly matching the independent measurement, and the file was restored byte-identical; new `audit03n-npc-registries.test.js` **6/6**, and **all 6 fail on the pre-fix file**, so neither behaviour test is vacuous; the NPC/audit family **45/45**; full Playwright **732 passed / 4 failed** = the documented `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` four and nothing else. **§7½ DONE — player-visible, and it is the point of the row:** at dear-friend favor the victory scroll now renders *"Quill settled the debt. When two lists of the same thing disagree, the one corroborated nowhere else is the wrong one. Docs: `story.md` §Epilogue (the consequence + the 3-row rename table), `world.md` §Cross-References, `prompt.md` §7 (13→14 gates + the exclusion), `index.md` doc map, and **dated KEY-DRIFT NOTES on the three lab reports whose design-time code blocks still show the surname keys** (annotate, don't rewrite — the §DX-02c/§AUDIT-03m precedent). **Side finding filed, not swept: §AUDIT-03o** — favor **3 is unreachable for 5 of the 6** NPCs, so the `entries[3]` epilogues this row just unlocked are still gated behind a tier nothing grants. Original row follows.
  **§AUDIT-03n (original) — four MORE registries key their entries to NPC names the favor ledger never spends (found 2026-07-31 during §AUDIT-03j; the npc-key twin of that row, and a measured extension of §AUDIT-03k).** §AUDIT-03j fixed `NODE_NPC_KEYS`/`NPC_FAREWELLS`, whose values were the profiles' SURNAMES rather than the profile keys. **The same three dead keys — `couperin` (=`quill`), `weckmann` (=`crov`), `bruhns` (=`auros`) — still key 12 entries across four more registries:** `NPC_EPILOGUES`, `NPC_NG_PLUS_GREETINGS`, `NPC_ACT_THREE_LINES`, `NPC_CROSS_REFS` (measured: 3 of 6 keys dead in each; `NPC_VOID_PRESSURE_LINES`/`NPC_NG_MEMORY_LINES`/`NPC_ROMANCE_PREAMBLES` are clean). **The sharpest consequence is in the ending:** the epilogue builder walks `npcOrder = ['yael','brynn','couperin','pachelbel','weckmann','bruhns']` and reads `S_story.npcFavorability[key]`, which is **always 0** for three of them — so the endgame renders the *stranger* variant for Quill, Weckmann and Bruhns no matter how close you got, while the fav≥2/fav≥3 lines authored for them are unreachable. (Note a sibling `npcOrder` at the void-pressure site already uses the real keys — the two lists disagree, which is the tell.) **Fix shape:** rename the keys to the profile keys in all four registries + the epilogue `npcOrder`, then extend `check:noderegs` with a 5th phase (or a sibling `check:npcregs`) asserting every npc-keyed registry key resolves in the 4-registry `npcKeyVocab()` — the npc mirror of gate #13, which would have caught all 12. **Risk:** low (renames + a gate); the lines already exist and are authored. **Verify:** an epilogue test at fav 3 for `quill`/`crov`/`auros` must render the dear-friend variant, not `entries[0]`.

Phases 1–2 only ever matched `^const`, which is the entire reason a 16-key table could rot for months inside a function while the gate reported green; the file has **3** such node tables (`CLUSTER`, `_vaSites`, `birkaNpcs` — the latter two already clean) and 2 compass ones (`ARROWS`/`DELTAS`, classified with a reason). **Evidence:** `check:walk` **16/16 exit 0**; `check:noderegs --selftest` **6/6**, the two new plants being a dead key in a classified local table *and* an unclassified one; **negative control** — the gate run against the HEAD `CLUSTER` fails **exit 1** naming exactly the eleven dead keys, restored green with an empty diff; new `audit03p-void-flavor-cluster.test.js` **10/10** (source: every key live, every value a real `CRACK` bucket, the three phantoms absent; render: the crack line reaches `LHR`+`BMA`+`LCY`+`WM`+`GVA` at pressure 3, the ≥6 named-place line reaches `LCY`; two controls — pressure 2 renders nothing, and `CDG`, in no cluster, stays silent at pressure 6), and a **positive control** proving the tests are not vacuous: run against HEAD, **6 of the 10 fail** and the 4 that pass are exactly the already-live `LHR`/`WM` cases. Only `LHR`, `STN`, `NUE`, `WM`, `MUC` resolve. The function returns `''` on a miss, so at void pressure ≥ 3 the Birka/Tilbury/Weimar/wild **flavor line silently never renders** at eleven of the sixteen places it was authored for — dead player-facing content, failing the way §AUDIT-03j's 42 references failed. **Why gate #13 (`check:noderegs`) is green anyway, and this is the interesting part:** it classifies and checks **top-level** registries; `CLUSTER` is declared *inside a function body*, so it is invisible to the scan. **`SL`/`CR`/`BA`/`DK`/`MQ`/`DF`/`MT`/`HM` remap mechanically** through `docs/maps/node-index.md`'s LEGACY CODE MAP (→ `BMA`/`KRN`/`LLA`/`LCY`/`LGW`/`ZRH`/`GVA`/`DUS`); **`SH`, `PH` and `MH` are in no map at all** and need identification from the cluster they were filed under, not a guess. **Fix shape:** remap the resolvable eight, classify the three unknowns explicitly, then extend gate #13 with a **function-local** node-keyed-object phase — the same widening §AUDIT-03n was to #13's registry list. **Do not batch this with the doc sweep**: it edits the game file (Hazard #1 — stop the server, commit early) and it is player-visible, so it needs §7½. **Risk:** low-medium. **Verify:** the eight remapped codes resolve; a void-pressure render at one repaired node shows the flavor line where it previously showed nothing; `check:walk` green with the new phase catching the pre-fix table as a negative control.
- [ ] **§AUDIT-03o — favor level 3 is unreachable for 5 of the 6 canonical NPCs, so the `entries[3]` tier is authored-but-dead content (found 2026-07-31 during §AUDIT-03n; needs a design call).** §AUDIT-03n unlocked the fav≥2 *and* fav≥3 lines for Quill/Weckmann/Bruhns by fixing their keys — but **measurement of every writer to the ledger shows level 3 is only ever granted to two characters.** The writers are exactly: `_setNpcFavor(key, level)` (whose literal call sites are `_setNpcFavor('yael', 3)`, `('crov',1)`, `('solvak',1)`, `('yva',1)`), the VM's `favor` opcode (corpus-wide the `set:` values are **only 1 and 2** — 10 × `set:1`, 4 × `set:2`), the dear-friend auto-upgrade (`npcFavorability[key] = 2`, both sites), and one hardcoded `npcFavorability['kenickie'] = 3`. **So `brynn`/`quill`/`pachelbel`/`crov`/`auros` cap at 2**, and everything gated on 3 is dead: their `NPC_EPILOGUES[3]` lines (the richest ending text in the file), `FROBERGER_TRACES.crov`'s `minFav:3`, and the `weckmann_class` arc-epilogue condition `_npcFavor('crov') >= 3`. **This is a design call, not an audit fix** — three honest readings: (a) tier 3 is *meant* to be a rare apex only Yael reaches, in which case the other five NPCs' `[3]` entries should be demoted to `[2]` or deleted rather than left as dead authoring; (b) each NPC deserves a third personal act that grants 3 (content work, and the natural companion to §NPC-01-D / §RIPPLE-01-C's "the favor track is too thin to earn its ladder"); or (c) the dear-friend auto-upgrade should promote to 3 when a *second* personal bit lands. **Do not default it** — (a) deletes authored content and (b) is an arc each. **Coordinate with §RIPPLE-01-C and §NPC-01-D**, which are already scoped to widen this same track. **Risk:** low to measure, medium to author. **Verify:** whatever the choice, a test asserting the top tier each NPC can actually reach matches the top tier its tables author.
- [x] **§AUDIT-03m ✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-04 `b399318` — `world.md` and `story.md` stop speaking 26×16, and the sweep is a measured tool with a gate rather than a one-off pass. The row's premise held; its warning about a blind replace was the whole design problem, and the sharpest finding was that the CODES were the symptom, not the disease.** **Measured first, never carried from the row:** a naive word-boundary scan over every `.md` returns **4,663 hits — and most are noise.** `DC` is a difficulty class **435** times, `EB` abbreviates *Epic Battleground* **163** times, and `IS`/`AT`/`CO`/`ST` sit inside ordinary English. So `scripts/legacy-codes.js` (`npm run legacy`) reads the LEGACY CODE MAP out of the **generated** `docs/maps/node-index.md` (§AUDIT-03l's gate #12 keeps it live — the map is never re-typed) and counts a token only in a **node context**, with the ambiguous codes classified **explicitly** — the §AUDIT-03j/n house style, for the same reason: a percentage heuristic is blind to a code that is *wholly* ambiguous. That took 4,663 → **2,517 real references, 230 of them in live docs.** **Every doc is classified SWEEP / PENDING / HISTORY, and an unclassified one FAILS** — four turned up on the first run and were classified by reading them. **HISTORY is annotated, never rewritten** (§DX-02c / §AUDIT-03n precedent): lab reports, `plan-archive.md`, BACKLOG and `maps.md`'s quarantined tables hold **2,287** references and are left alone — the tool **refuses** `--annotate` on one, and a test proves it. **Shipped:** `world.md` **77 annotations / 81 lines** and `story.md` **193 / 160**, in two forms because one form cannot serve both cases — sparse lines get inline `` `LHR` (historical `CI`) ``, a run of 3+ codes gets live codes plus **one** trailing key (`*(historical: `CI`=`LHR` · …)*`), since ten interleaved parentheticals is a wall, not an annotation. **The finding that mattered more than the codes:** `world.md`'s node sections describe a **retired navigation model**, and annotating them alone would have laundered a false claim into live-looking codes. Measured at HEAD: **0 of the 416 live nodes carries an `N`/`S`/`E`/`W` field** (§CELL-01 stripped them), and `J1`/`J6`/`J7` are absent — so *"Node connections from CI (**verified against `NODE_MAP`**): N→SL · E→IN"* was true when written and is not a claim anyone can re-check. It now carries a **historical-model banner** naming all three retirements (edge graph · junction stubs · the `R03,C16` 26×16 projection, live coords being r 2–73 / c 154–249) and pointing at `./api.sh location <CODE>` as the authority; the junction-vignette paragraph was rewritten to say the stubs are **deleted** and that §AUDIT-03j rehomed seven of them. **Three tool bugs the review caught before they shipped, each a real one:** it wrote **inside fenced code blocks** (12 lines of a `world.md` milepoint block), it **nested backticks** on already-backticked codes (```` ``J2` (historical …)` ````, 11 lines), and it missed a **single-code table cell** (`| Yael | CI | … |`). All three are fixed in the tool and pinned by tests — the output was re-generated from a clean `git checkout`, never hand-patched around. **Evidence:** `check:walk` **16/16 exit 0** (new gate #16 `check:legacycodes`); **negative control on the real doc** — a bare *"north of SL"* appended to `world.md` turned the gate **red (exit 1)**, restored with `shasum -c` **OK** and an empty `git diff`; new `audit03m-legacy-codes.test.js` **7/7**, of which **two are controls**: a **positive** control feeding six shapes that must still be caught (a heading run · a direction sentence · `Node CI` · an arrow list · a table cell · a bare code on an explanatory line) and a **negative** control feeding nine that must not be (`DC 10` · *bait search DC reduced* · *EB engagement audit* · `Q-TL-01` · an already-annotated code · a dense-form legend · a backticked code on an explanatory line · a table header · ordinary English) — because **a detector narrowed into uselessness and a deleted detector look identical in a green run** (§DX-02l-FU's lesson, applied deliberately). Full Playwright **778 passed / 4 failed / 0 flaky** = the documented `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` four and nothing else (771 + these 7). **No HTML touched, no §7½** — docs + tooling only; the WBAPI server stayed stopped (no writes needed). **Two limits, asserted rather than left to be discovered:** an ambiguous code is flagged only on a node-context line (precision over recall — the price of not mangling English), and **a fenced block is code**, so a dead code inside a *runnable example* escapes the gate. One did: `docs/notes/docs-dev-environment.md`'s `./api.sh get node CY` — a copy-pasteable command against a node that has not existed for months — **fixed by hand to `HKG`**, and the class is recorded here rather than papered over. **Open tail: the 7 PENDING live docs (230 references)**, reported by name and count on every gate run — `docs/notes/docs-node-network.md` 95 · `story-arc-epic-battlegrounds` 41 · `story-arc-investigation` 27 · `story-arc-npc-dialogues` 22 · `story-arc-coastal` 13 · `story-flowchart` 12 · `story-arc-ngplus` 8. They are PENDING, not SWEEP, because **a gate that fails on work nobody has done yet is a red light you learn to ignore**; moving a file to SWEEP is the next slice and needs no design call. **Side finding filed, not swept: §AUDIT-03p** — a *live engine* table with the same rot, which gate #13 cannot see. Original row follows. **§AUDIT-03m — the prose still speaks 26×16 (found 2026-07-29 during §AUDIT-03l).** §AUDIT-03l fixed the *tables an author looks a code up in*; the legacy codes still appear in **running prose and cross-references** across `world.md`, `story.md`, `docs/story/*`, `docs/notes/*` and the lab reports (`"N of CI"`, `"east of DS"`, `Q-FR-01`-style design IDs, etc.). **Deliberately not swept here:** a blind token replace over prose is how you turn `AT`/`IS`/`SE`/`CA` into nonsense inside English sentences, and the **design IDs (`Q-TL-01`, `Q-FR-02`, §XIX/§XX arc names) are identifiers and must NOT be remapped.** Do it per-document, reading each hit, with `docs/maps/node-index.md`'s LEGACY CODE MAP as the key — and prefer *annotating* (`CI` → `` `LHR` (historical `CI`) ``) over silent replacement so the older records stay readable. **Risk:** low; **value:** medium (the authoring trap itself is closed by 03l's gate — this is legibility, not correctness).

- [x] **§DX-02f — the two undocumented Playwright reds ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-30. Both were stale tests; both mechanics measured CORRECT at HEAD, so the §XP-01 and §BOARD-01-FU8 ship records stand.** The full suite is now **710 passed / 4 failed** — the red set is the `worldbuilder-crud-arrays` four and nothing else. **The row's "deterministic, not flaky" claim was wrong, and that was the tell.** `effort-xp:67` forced its failed check with `Math.random = () => 0.01`, but **§VM-01-B had moved `_rollSkill`'s d20 onto the seeded stream** (`QuestRuntime._rollSkill` → `E.rng()`, `22104`) — the stub controlled nothing, the check rolled freely, and the test failed exactly when the roll *passed* and paid the full `150` instead of the 38 effort share. Measured live: 3 bare single-attempt runs went **fail / pass / fail** (quest_muffat_01 is DEX Stealth DC 13; at DEX 8 the total is `d20+1`, so it's a ~45% coin flip — the earlier "reproduces on a bare re-run" reading came from the 2-retry config, not from determinism). `warrants-board:999` was subtler: **every standing assertion already passed** (ESCALATED `+4`, idempotent re-credit, `questDone`) — the only failure was `posAfter ≠ posBefore`, because the test hand-set `playerR/C = 5,9` while setting `currentCode = 'RVP'`, a state no step can produce. `storyRender`'s §CELL-03 sync (`31618`) then corrected the coords to RVP's real cell (17,180) and the test read that correction as a warp. **No jump travel, no player move — the invariant it guards is intact.** **Shipped (test-only, no HTML):** `effort-xp` now forces the fail through the engine's own dial — a scoped `forceFail(id, fn)` helper that raises `sc.dc` to 999 and restores it in a `finally` — the same idiom `warrants-board` itself already used for a forced PASS (`sc.dc = -100`); `warrants-board` derives the stand-on cell from `NODE_COORDS[q.activateNode]` instead of hardcoding one. **Evidence:** both suites **28/28 green on three consecutive runs with `--retries=0`** (so no flake masking); full Playwright **710 passed / 4 failed**, the four being the documented §DX-02d crud-arrays set; `check:walk` **12/12 exit 0**; `git diff --stat` = 2 test files, +21/−7, `roll2hit-v3.html` untouched (no §7½ — nothing a player sees changed). **Durable fix (the row's actual ask, §DX-02c's proposal): prompt.md §7 now records the FULL red set — four, in one file — with the date last re-proved**, plus the two lessons: *a test that stubs an RNG must stub the stream the code actually draws*, and (again, after §DX-02e) *pin the property, never a fabricated or generated coordinate*. **Original row:** A full `npx playwright test` is **708 passed / 6 failed**, not the 4 that §DX-02d documents. The two extras: `effort-xp.test.js:67` (*§XP-01 — failed skill-check grants 25% of reward XP, once per quest*, asserting at `:91`) and `warrants-board.test.js:999` (*§BOARD-01-FU8 — completing a Void hunt accrues the ESCALATED standing at the real onPass site*). **Both proved pre-existing at HEAD by stash-diff on 2026-07-30** (identical failures with the §DX-01c working tree stashed) and both are deterministic, not flaky — they reproduce on a bare re-run. Neither is in prompt.md §7, which still claims exactly ONE carried red. **This is the second time in three sessions a session has burned a stash-diff re-proving an undocumented red** (§DX-02d made the same complaint about the crud-arrays four) — so the durable fix is the one §DX-02c proposes: either fix them, or record the *full* red set in prompt.md §7 with the date it was last re-proved. Diagnose first — both touch shipped mechanics (effort XP, Warrant standing), so a genuine regression that predates this session is very much possible and would matter more than the bookkeeping. **Risk:** unknown until diagnosed (docs-only if they're stale tests; real if the mechanic drifted).
- [x] **§DX-02e ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-29 — the road was never broken; the test pinned generated coordinates. Verdict: the §KG design promise holds, and the assertion now proves the property instead of the cells.** The row asked which is truth — re-lay the corridor or update the samples. **Measured, not guessed:** auto-travel routes with `_roadGridPathCore` (§NAV-01d road-weighted Dijkstra, road/lane = cost 1, else 2), and every one of the six legs `TLL→SPB→KMS→ZVD→FBR→TVR→SVO` walks **entirely on road** today — `TLL(10,204)→9,204→8,204…8,210→9,210→SPB(10,210)` (the §DX-01a row-8 reroute through TUO, off the Gulf-of-Finland sea cell `10,207`), then the SE staircase `10,211 · 11,212 · 11,213 · 12,214 · 12,215 · 13,216 · 13,217`. **Zero intermediate cells off-road on any leg**, so *"a player currently rolls encounters where the lab report says they shouldn't"* was **false** — nobody was ever rolling. Re-laying the old row-10/11 run was therefore rejected on a second ground too: `ROAD_RUNS` is **generated** (`scripts/build-roads.js — do not hand-edit`), so hand-restored cells die at the next `--apply`. **Shipped:** `kg-zones.test.js` "the corridor is real road" rewritten to call the engine's own router per leg and assert every intermediate cell is `ROAD_CELLS` or a settlement cell, with the full route printed in the failure message. **Evidence:** red reproduced first (1 failed / 4 passed, `10,205 is a road cell`); after — **kg-zones 5/5**, `navigation` + `minimap-nav` **36/36**, `audit03e-node-code` **7/7**, `check:walk` **11/11 exit 0**. **Negative control (temp test, deleted):** deleting `11,212`/`11,213` from the live `ROAD_CELLS` made the router detour `12,211 · 12,212` and the assertion reported both — it is not vacuous. **Docs:** the §KG lab report §2 gained a dated route-drift note (the §DX-02c template: the report's cell ranges are the build-time lay, not the live net); `prompt.md` §7 drops to **ONE** carried Playwright red (`worldbuilder-crud-arrays`, §DX-02d). **No HTML touched, no §7½ — this is a test + docs increment; the player-facing claim was verified through the real router in a live page load.** **The general lesson, recorded in prompt.md §7: never pin generated data by coordinate — pin the property it was generated to satisfy.** Original row follows.
  **§DX-02e (original) — `kg-zones.test.js` "the corridor is real road" is a carried Playwright red (found 2026-07-29 during §AUDIT-03e).** The test samples 6 cells the §KG lab report §2 documents as the SPB→TVR road; **4 of them are no longer in `ROAD_CELLS`** (`10,205`, `10,209`, `11,210`, `14,216` — only `11,212`/`12,214` survive). Proved pre-existing at HEAD on 2026-07-29 (identical result against `git show HEAD:roll2hit-v3.html` with zero working-tree changes), so it is **not** an §AUDIT-03e regression. Almost certainly a §DX-01a leftover: `build-roads.js --apply` rerouted TLL→SPB off the Gulf-of-Finland sea cell 10,207 via the row-8 TUO corridor and did not re-lay the old row-10/11 run. Decide which is truth — re-lay the corridor with `build-roads.js --apply` (the §KG design promises encounter-free travel between the zones, so a player currently rolls encounters where the lab report says they shouldn't) or update the test's sample cells to the new route — then clear it from prompt.md §7. Note this is the exact class §DX-02c proposes a rule for, and §DX-01a's ship record did not mention the test. **Risk:** low.

> **✅ Multiplayer IS shown on the world map — re-verified 2026-07-12 at HEAD.** §MP-MAP (2026-07-08) renders remote players as **cyan markers on all three map panes**: Local grid `☺` (`_renderMapGrid` ~`35606`, from `MP.nearby`), World canvas (`_paintWorldWindow` ~`35431`, from `MP.remotes`), Full canvas + the story-bar GLOBE preview (`_paintFullWorld` ~`35318`) — each drawn *before* the local `@`/red so the player always wins a shared cell. - [ ] **§MESH-01-REVIEW — remaining audit items:**
  - **`nearby`+`world[]` are pos-only** — the `room` half of the surface asymmetry closed with §NAV-01f (`buildLook` shared on start/move/look/pos); fold presence arrays into a shared builder only if a MUD client ever needs them on `move`/`look`. Minor.
  - [x] **Presence Playwright tests share one server ✅ CLOSED 2026-07-07** — the flake root: all 7 tests in `multiplayer-presence.test.js` share the ONE throwaway server, and `ctx.close()`/disconnect fire only `player_left` — the session itself lingers in `SESSIONS` to the 30-min TTL, so a leaked session bled a phantom pid/name into the next test's `/api/session/who`. Fixes: (1) **`test.afterEach` sweep** — GET `/api/session/who` → POST `/api/session/end` for every local session id, so each test starts clean (guarded `try/catch` for the afterAll window when the server is already gone). (2) **Assert by pid, never by count** — the reload ghost-check was `who.sessions.filter(name==='Odys')).toHaveLength(1)` (a raw count a cross-test leak inflates); now captures `pid = MP.pid` (stable across resume, `pid = pidOf(sid)`) and asserts `who.sessions.filter(name==='Odys').map(s=>s.pid)` `toEqual([pid])` — a ghost surfaces a *second, different* pid under the name; a leak can inflate a count but never forge a pid. Verified: `multiplayer-presence` **7/7** in 6.9 s (server-independent; dev :1367 restarted after). Test-file-only; no product code touched.
  - **Refactors:** if `MESH.log` pressure ever shows, derive `moved` from `arrived` at fanout instead of a third event per move. *(✅ 2026-07-06 client pair SHIPPED: `_mpRepaintMaps` 100 ms trailing debounce — SSE bursts coalesce into one repaint, MP.on re-checked at fire time; `_mpBase()` defaults to `location.origin` on http(s) pages so a world served by a friend connects back to that server, `file://` keeps the localhost:1367 dev default, explicit `localStorage.mpServer` still wins.)* *(✅ 2026-07-06 `mesh.js` extraction SHIPPED: the 434-line §MESH-01 kernel — ACL · rate limit · gossip · tracker/federation/bootstrap, the block between the old §MESH sentinels — moved verbatim to `mesh.js` as a factory `require('./mesh')(deps)`; the server passes live surfaces (SESSIONS, SSE fanout, chat ring, manifest/serverId, ledger hooks) and destructures the same names back so all endpoint call sites are unchanged; the one outside write to the internal `_trackerDirty` flag became `trackerMarkDirty()`. `wbapi-server.js` 11,726→11,314 lines. Gates: mud-harness 251/251 · mesh Playwright 43/43 · check:walk 6/6 · live boot + `./api.sh mesh status` verified. Docs synced: index.md file table + docs/notes/docs-node-network.md §12.)*
